# Valhalla game TODO

Pick any task by its **ID** (for example, `I65-MAP` or `A70-MAGE`). Each checkbox is one deliverable, and its ID stays stable if the list is reordered. Use focused private checks for the chosen change. An instance entrance stays sealed until its `OPEN` task is complete.

Rules for every TODO: use the `createmap` skill for all zone work; create all sprites through the PixelFlow API; create all music through the dybase2 MCP. Read `docs/musical-style.md` before making or changing any song.

All earlier tasks are finished (list cleared 2026-10-21; the full list is in git history, last version at commit f388e72).

- [ ] `LEVEL-CAP-99` — Owner (2026-11-13): "level 99 is the highest level". Today `Character::grant_xp` (`server/src/model.rs`) loops while `self.level < 1000`, and `xp_to_level` extrapolates past the table (+2300 per level), so a level-99 hero keeps levelling to 100+. Add one `MAX_LEVEL: u32 = 99` constant and use it everywhere: `grant_xp` stops at 99 and **XP no longer accumulates at 99** (`xp` stays 0, `levels` 0); rested reserve is not consumed at 99 (`Rested::bonus` / `reward_xp`); debug/GM `set_level` (`world.rs`, `world/gm_test.rs`, client `/level`) clamps to 1..=99; the client XP bar shows full with a "Max level" label instead of `0 / N` (`client/game.js` HUD, Swedish by hand). Check whether any saved character is above 99 before release (2026-11-13: highest is 99, none above) and, if any are, clamp them in a one-way load migration. Keep rebirth (`docs/rebirth.md`), enemy levels (`VALHALLA_LEVEL_SPREAD` can roll 101 for a 99 enemy: leave enemies alone unless the owner says otherwise), and `levels.txt` unchanged. Checks: Rust test that a level-99 hero given XP stays 99 with 0 XP and an unspent rested reserve; update `level_table_drives_xp...` only if its `xp_to_level(99)` assertion is removed; focused UI check of the max-level XP bar. Server release; `Beta` patch bump + release note.
- [ ] `GM-TP-PLAYER` — Owner (2026-11-13): "gm should be able to teleport to a player. like /tp Funter would teleport to where funter is". The server side already exists: `/goto <player>` (`client/gm.js`) sends `DebugCommand::Goto` (`server/src/world/debug.rs`), which finds an online player by exact, case-insensitive name in any zone, moves the GM there (collision-adjusted, `hidden` for GM characters) and replies `{zone,x,y}`. Only `/tp` needs to accept a name: in `COMMANDS.tp` (`client/gm.js`), when the arguments are not `x y` numbers and `findZone(a[0])` finds no zone, send `{ op: 'goto', name: a.join(' ') }` (multi-word names too). Zone numbers and zone names keep priority. If an exact player name collides with a fuzzy zone match, check `findZone` and prefer an exact zone match, else the player. Update the `tp` usage/help text (`<zone> [x y] | <x y> | <player>`), `/gmcommands`, and the "Unknown zone" error to mention players ("No zone or online player named …"). Client only, no server change. Check that the goto lands in a dungeon/raid copy when the target is inside one (the copy's zone id, not the template); if it doesn't, fix `Goto` (that would make it a server release). Checks: a focused `tests/gm-ui.cjs` case (needs a server without test commands; two characters, `/tp <other>` lands within 1 tile in the other's zone, an unknown name shows the error, `MUTATE=1` must fail), Swedish by hand for new strings, `Beta` patch bump + release note.
- [ ] `CUTSCENE-SKIP-SPARK-TRAVEL` — Owner (2026-11-13): "the cutscenes shouldn't be played when spark traveling." `CUTSCENE-RULES` already specified that crossing cutscenes (`sky_up`, `sky_down`, `dive`, `surface`) must never play on Spark Travel crossings or landings, only on real portal crossings. Currently in `server/src/world/travel.rs` (`update_spark`), crossing a zone or landing sets `p.portal_at = self.time` (and landing clears `p.character.spark_travel = None` before `update_lead_shoes` runs), so `crossing_scenes` in `server/src/world/cutscene.rs` sees `time - p.portal_at <= CROSSING_WINDOW` (`0.3`) and fires unseen crossing cutscenes during or right at the end of a Spark flight (and records them in `Character.cutscenes`). Fix `crossing_scenes` / `update_spark` so neither mid-flight Spark zone crossings nor Spark landings trigger cutscenes or mark them as seen (while still keeping `PORTAL_DELAY` protection on landing if needed), and add focused Rust tests in `server/src/world/cutscene_tests.rs`.

- [x] `EARLY-LEVEL-TUTORIAL` — **Live Beta 1.51.0 (2026-11-13 21:04 CET, deployed to `valhalla.service`).** Added non-blocking amber glow + anchored tooltip arrow tutorial (`client/tutorial.js`, `client/online.css`, `client/settings.js`, `client/online.js`, `client/field.js`, `client/index.html`) for the 6 onboarding milestones (`first_quest`, `levelup_stats` including `★` + `Recommended`, `equip_gear`, `new_skill`, `consumables`, `merchant`) with server persistence/one-way >level 5 migration (`server/src/model.rs`, `server/src/store.rs`, `server/src/world.rs`, `server/src/visibility.rs`), `"Got it"` / `"Disable tutorial"` buttons, Settings `"Reset / Replay tutorial"`, touch control targeting (`#touch-stick`, `#touch-actions`, `#touch-quickuse`, `#bag-character`), cutscene/boss/death/ghost suppression, Swedish translations (`client/sv-data.js`, `scripts/build_swedish.py`), and release notes (`Beta 1.51.0`). Verified with `cargo test tutorial` (2 passed), `python3 scripts/build_swedish.py --check`, `node tests/whats-new-notes.cjs`, `tests/tutorial-ui.cjs` (16 checks, desktop + touch + reload persistence on release binary), and `MUTATE=1` failing as expected. Original entry: Owner (2026-11-13): "make a somewhat tutorial for the first few levels. like when you level up. make a glow around the character pane and then glow around the stats tab and show tooltips on what to do. also when getting equipment. show them what to do etc etc." + "also buying and selling. buying consumables etc etc". **Decided (ask round, 2026-11-13):**
  - **Milestones (trigger once per character the first time each event occurs; no hard level cap):**
    1. **First quest, movement & combat**: after landing / stepping off the Greenmeadow pier, brief guidance on moving, targeting/attacking, and accepting/tracking the first quest (`#quest-tracker`; on touch, `#touch-stick` and `#touch-actions` Attack/Quests).
    2. **Level-up & stat points**: when leveling up with unspent `statPoints`, glow `#inventory-open` (or touch `Bags`), then once `#equipment` opens glow `#character-tab-stats` (`Character & stats`, or `#bag-character` when in bags-only/narrow view), then glow `#character-attributes` pointing out the class's `★` specialty stat (`+`) and the `Recommended` button ("Click `+` on your `★` stat or use `Recommended`").
    3. **Getting & equipping new gear**: when receiving/looting a compatible non-starter gear piece, glow `#inventory-open` (or touch `Bags`), then glow that item in `#inventory-list` with a tooltip explaining how to click/drag it onto the paper-doll slot to equip.
    4. **New skill unlocked**: on unlocking a new active skill on level-up, glow `#skills-open` (`K`) (or touch skills / `Other skills`), then explain arranging/swapping skills in `#skills-panel` and using `#skillbar`.
    5. **Consumables**: on looting/receiving the first food or potion, show a tooltip anchored to `#quickuse` (`Z` food / `X` health potion / `C` mana potion, or `#touch-quickuse` on touch) explaining quick-use and shared cooldowns.
    6. **Merchant buying & selling**: on opening a Merchant shop window (`#npc-dialogue` / `client/shop.js`) for the first time, glow the Buy/Sell controls and explain buying food/potions for recovery and selling unneeded loot for gold.
  - **Server persistence & migration**: store completed tutorial milestone IDs and a `tutorial_disabled` flag on `Character` (`server/src/model.rs`, `server/src/store.rs`, `#[serde(default)]` one-way migration) with a client-to-server sync message to mark steps done, disable, or reset. **Existing characters above level 5** at migration/load auto-mark basic onboarding milestones as done so veterans aren't prompted, while brand-new characters get every milestone once regardless of level.
  - **Presentation & UX safeguards**:
    - **Non-blocking gold/amber glow + anchored tooltip arrow**: gameplay stays interactive (no full-screen dimmed modal); clicking or completing the highlighted action advances the step.
    - **Controls**: each tooltip has `"Got it"` / dismiss and `"Disable tutorial"` buttons; Settings (`#settings-panel`) gets a `"Reset / Replay tutorial"` control.
    - **Touch/mobile (`(pointer: coarse)` / narrow screens)**: automatically target touch controls (`#touch-actions` Bags/Attack/Quests, `#touch-stick`, `#touch-quickuse`, `#touch-skills`, and `#bag-character`) and keep tooltips clear of the thumbstick and edges.
    - **Interruptions**: pause/hide tutorial overlays during cutscenes (`Cutscene`), boss fights, or death/ghost form (`hero.dead` / `hero.ghost`) so they never overlap, resuming cleanly once clear.
    - **Localization & tests**: hand-written Swedish (`scripts/sv-*.txt` + `python3 scripts/build_swedish.py`), `Beta` version bump + release note; focused Rust tests for character save/migration (> level 5 skip) and sync ops, plus a bounded Chromium test (`MUTATE=1` fails) covering desktop + touch flows.
- [x] `ESCORT-COMBAT-ASSIST` — **Implemented and checked 2026-11-13 (server source, not yet live-released; no version bump or release note yet).** An escort fights any enemy within 14 tiles that has picked it or its hero (`ASSIST` in `server/src/world/escort.rs`): it leaves the route, targets it, uses its class skills through `ai_combat`, moves at its class speed and paths round walls like a hired fighter, then walks on. Tests `cargo test escort_assist`, `cargo test eydis_walks`. `enemies_pick_the_escort_as_a_target_and_hurt_her` fails (target selection, untouched code; not compared against HEAD). Original entry: Owner: "escort quests, the escorted person shouldn't just continue walking. they should help killing the mobs etc. if any of the player or the escorted gets aggro the escorted should start attacking". In `server/src/world/escort.rs`, an escorted NPC currently either continues walking along its waypoints ignoring combat or stands frozen without defending itself when an enemy is within 3.5 tiles. Update escort AI so that if an enemy targets or damages either the escort OR the escort's player owner, the escort stops advancing along the route, acquires the threat, and actively attacks using their class combat abilities to help defeat the mobs. Once all nearby threats targeting them or the player are eliminated, the escort resumes walking their path.

- [x] `OUTPOST-GUARDS` — **Live Beta 1.50.0 (2026-11-13, deployed to `valhalla.service`).** Dynamic Outpost Guards (`server/src/world/guards.rs`, `server/src/world.rs`, `client/field.js`) now patrol every open-world town and camp in hostile zones (2 guards per hub, 90 hubs across all zones), scaled cleanly to each zone's upper level with standard gear and zero potion stacks. Guards engage nearby or lured enemies, fight with Warrior abilities inside a localized post tether, respawn after 25 seconds if defeated, return to post when clear, do not trivialize elites, and suppress player XP, gold, loot drops, kills, and quest/achievement credit when killing or heavily assisting (including any elite damage). Original entry: Owner: "every outpost should have guards. so if an enemy comes too close they'll kill it. however. they shouldn't be too overpowered". Station guard NPCs at each outpost / camp across the zones. If an enemy enters or is lured near the outpost perimeter, guards will engage and kill the hostile creature to defend the camp. Guard combat stats should be balanced to the zone level (sturdy enough to defeat stray monsters without being overpowered, one-shotting foes, or enabling elite/boss exploit kills), with a localized guard tether so they return to their posts once the threat is cleared.
- [x] `CORPSE-RUN-GHOST-RESPAWN` — Owner: "When you die. your body should be in the place where it dies. you should spawn as a ghost and you have to get to your body to resurect at half hp". Currently in `server/src/world.rs`, dying holds the player down for 3.2 seconds and then directly teleports them to the camp spawn at full HP. Replace this with a corpse run mechanic: (1) on death, the player's corpse remains on the ground at the death location; (2) the player spawns at the nearest graveyard / rally camp as a ghost (translucent spirit form, peaceful ghost state, with a corpse direction indicator on the minimap/HUD); (3) the player must navigate back to their corpse location; (4) reaching the corpse allows resurrecting back into physical form at the corpse location at **half HP** (`50% max_hp`). Done on `feat/corpse-run` (`Beta 1.49.0`): translucent ghost (`globalAlpha *= 0.45`), single-pass bleak grayscale world + drifting fog (`ghostCv`, `drawGhostFog`), corpse direction arrow + minimap/WorldMap markers, `nearest_graveyard` respawn, peaceful ghost state, and half-HP resurrection at corpse; verified with focused Rust tests + `tests/corpse-run-ui.cjs` (`REAL_SPRITES=1` + `MUTATE=1`).
- [x] `ENEMY-CHASE-CLOSE-RANGE` — **Implemented and checked 2026-11-13 (server source, not yet live-released).** In `server/src/world.rs`: (1) `update_slime` now keeps open-world enemies chasing past `HOME_LEASH_RANGE` (`14.`) while the target remains within `CLOSE_CHASE_RANGE` (`9.`), returning home only once the target leaves close range past the home leash (while dungeons and raids via `hunting` keep unlimited chase as before); (2) `walk_with_actors` allows an already-overlapped enemy (`same_cell` or `< radius + r`) to step away from the overlap toward its chase target instead of freezing in place, without stepping closer onto an actor. Verified with 6 focused Rust tests (`open_world_enemy_keeps_chasing_past_home_leash_when_target_is_close_and_returns_when_far`, `dungeon_and_raid_enemies_keep_unlimited_chase_past_close_and_home_ranges`, `enemy_walks_out_of_player_overlap_when_chasing`, `outdoor_enemies_still_drop_aggro_at_their_normal_leash`, `melee_monsters_wind_up_from_the_longer_reach_and_not_beyond`, `instance_pursuit_retargets_after_death_but_never_crosses_private_copies`). Owner: "enemy chasing should not stop if they're close enough." In `server/src/world.rs` (`step_slime`), an enemy currently breaks off chase and switches to `"return"` once it reaches its home leash distance (`distance(home) >= 14.`) or target distance (`d >= 9.`), causing enemies to abruptly reset and retreat even when actively fighting a player right next to them. Furthermore, during active chase, ensure enemies do not halt or stutter pursuit when close to the target (adjusting body collision / reach boundaries). Rule: when an enemy is close enough to the player (e.g. actively engaged in combat or within melee/close proximity), chasing must persist and not leash back or stop.

Spark travel graph links and routes (`server/src/world/travel.rs`, `scripts/spark_travel.py`, `world/map.txt`) were authored on the older linear zone progression before the world map was reshaped with interconnected province boundaries and seams. Audit the travel station network against the current world geography, update travel links between adjacent hubs, and recalculate the fastest / shortest-path routes between all stations to eliminate circuitous detours while ensuring every direct leg has valid zone gate/seam crossings.
- [x] `NIGHT-LOOK-DISTINCT` — **Done 2026-11-13 (Beta 1.49.3):** `client/daynight.js` now layers theme-tuned cool moonlight `multiply` grading with a half-resolution darkness veil (`veilCv`) and edge vignette while cutting warm light pools around lamps/braziers/torches/campfires/cottages (plus additive `'lighter'` lantern glow) and gently easing the veil around the hero and active target; `client/city.js` (`drawNpcPlate`) and `client/field.js` defer NPC/enemy/player/corpse nameplates, HP bars, quest marks, gear labels, click markers, ward soak readouts and speech bubbles until after `DayNight.draw` so UI and combat feedback stay crisp. Owner: "make the night look more like night. as it is now. i can hardly see the difference between night and day". Outdoor night tint in `client/daynight.js` currently caps darkness at `darkAlpha = .27 * n * outdoorBlend * amount` with palette `rgba(18, 29, 67, ...)`. The subtle 0.27 alpha wash leaves outdoor scenes almost as bright as daytime, giving little night atmosphere and making lamp/fire glow blend into the ground. Increase night darkness depth and contrast across outdoor themes so nighttime is distinctly recognizable, while keeping actors, ground indicators, combat readability, and the `Prefs.night` Settings slider functional.

- [x] `ZONE-CONTEXT-UNLOAD` — **Implemented and checked 2026-11-13 (`Beta 1.52.1`).** `client/field.js` keeps a context only for the hero's zone, its seam/vault neighbours and the last two zones visited (`dropCtx`, `Field.ctxInfo`); `client/city.js` frees scenery sprites not drawn during the last two stays (`City.sweepSprites`); `client/music_player.js` releases a stopped score's decoded buffer after 60 s (`window.__musicReleaseS` in tests) — the music was the biggest holder (16 scores / 496 MB held after eight zones). `tests/zone-unload-ui.cjs` tours eight zones in the bounded runner (54 checks, under the cap); `MUTATE=ctx|sprites|music` each fail. Original entry: Owner (2026-11-13): "isn't the zones unloaded when you leave them?" They are not. In `client/field.js` every zone the hero enters, or that draws beside a seam, gets a context in the `ctxs` map (`ctxFor`: built map, objects, night lights and its own ground-chunk cache of up to 40 canvases), and nothing ever deletes one. Memory grows with every zone visited in a session; a long Spark flight crosses several, and the bounded test browser (1.5 GiB) dies of `oom-kill` after three zones plus a flight. Keep only the current zone, its seam and vault neighbours and the last one or two visited; release the chunk canvases of the rest explicitly (set width and height to 0, as the chunk eviction does) and rebuild a context on return. Check the world minimap's `miniCache` (separate, already capped) and that a seam crossing still swaps pointers without a rebuild. Prove it with a bounded run that visits five or more zones and stays under the cap, and that fails with the unloading switched off.
- [x] `SPARK-TRAVEL-MAP` — **Done 2026-11-13 (Beta 1.52.0):** the travel master opens the world map in travel mode (`WorldMap.showTravel`), `tests/spark-travel-ui.cjs` 33 checks, `docs/spark-travel.md`. Talking to a Travel Master currently presents Spark Travel destinations as text list buttons in the NPC conversation panel (`client/city.js`). Instead, open a map interface (using the world map) showing all known Spark stations across the world with travel beacon / spark icons. Hovering (or tapping on touch devices) a station displays its name, route/stops, availability, and gold cost. Clicking an available station initiates the flight (`Online.send({ type: 'interact', npc: npc.id, offer: 'spark:' + destination.id })`).
- [x] `LAMP-LIGHT-ANCHOR` — **Done 2026-11-13 (Beta 1.49.5):** Added per-kind emitter anchor offsets (`LIGHT_ANCHORS` / `lightAnchor` in `client/daynight.js`) applied before viewport culling, veil `destination-out` cutouts, and additive `glowSprite` placement (`lamp` at `[0, -120]`, zone lanterns/lamps at their bulb centers including `[19, -127]` for `ew_lantern` and `[0, -194]` for `year_lantern`, `brazier` `[0, -68]`, `torch` `[0, -88]`, `campfire` `[0, -24]`, `lamphouse` `[0, -164]`, and cottages/houses/mills at their window/facade heights, with `[0, -46]` fallback when `kind` is omitted). Verified: `tests/night-distinct-ui.cjs` (63 checks + `MUTATE=anchor` fails), `tests/daynight-ui.cjs` (12 checks), `tests/whats-new-notes.cjs`, and live night screenshots (`night-meadow.png`, `night-ember.png`, `night-fen.png`). Owner: "make the glowing lights really come from the lamp posts. now they're stemming from the middle of the pillar". The outdoor night lighting in `client/daynight.js` draws radial glow sprites at `(x - 64, y - 110)` (sprite center `[64, 64]`), placing the glow center at `y - 46` relative to the object's ground base. In `client/city.js`, a `lamp` post pillar stands from `[0, 0]` to `[0, -106]`, and the lantern enclosure sits at `y = -108` to `-133` (roof peak `-147`, center ≈ `-120`). Consequently, the glowing light currently stems from halfway up the post shaft instead of the lantern head. Align the glow vertical emitter offset to match the lamp post lantern head (center ≈ `y - 120`), and ensure other night light kinds (braziers, torches, campfires, cottages) use appropriate emitter heights rather than a flat `y - 46` offset.
- [x] `HELM-DIRECTIONS-PROPER` — **Done 2026-11-13, Beta 1.48.1 (route 1):** `turnHead` wraps each pose's front and back drawings round the head, with ears/feathers/horns as single standing parts; all five classes pass `tests/headgear-directions.cjs`, sheets reviewed with `tests/gear-sheet.cjs`; details and known limits in `docs/todo-feedback-hats.md`. Original entry: Owner (2026-11-13): "lots of equipment helms are not drawn correctly in several directions. like the hardened wolfhood. its good south and north. the rest look like shit. probably more heads have that"; then "fix the helms correctly". Diagnosis (contact sheets through the real compositor, hunter `warden`, `scout`, `ironhide`): every head layer has only two drawings, a front and a back; SW, W, E and SE reuse the FRONT drawing on a profile head, NW and NE reuse the back. The Beta 1.47.2 `HAT-DIRECTION-FIX` (`turnHead` in `client/magesprite.js`, `headFrames` in the class manifests) only narrows and shears that front drawing, which reads worse than leaving it alone. This affects all five classes and both bodies (382 native head layers plus their recolours), not just the Wolfhood. A correct fix shows a real side view: the face opening on the facing side, the back of the hood or helm covering the back of the head, side features (ears, feathers, horns, cheek guards) where a turned head would have them, clean outlines. Two routes: (1) wrap the saved front and back drawings round the head in the compositor (per row a radius about the head axis, a shift by the yaw, far-side features drawn behind the head, seam and silhouette outlines repaired), no sprite files changed; (2) author true side and three-quarter drawings per head piece in the gear pipeline. Judge either on eight-direction contact sheets for every class before release, all clips including hurt and death; replace `turnHead` rather than stacking on it; keep `tests/headgear-directions.cjs` meaningful (its "changed from the stored frame" check does not prove a hat looks right). Sheet tool: `tests/gear-sheet.cjs` ( `CLS`, `GENDER`, `LOOKS`, `NOHEAD=1` switches the 1.47.2 turning off).

Contact sheet (hunter `travel`, `hooded`, `mantle` with armour and pants): seen from behind (NW, N, NE) the cloak is drawn but the back, arms and legs show through and around it; from the front and sides it is a thin strip. A cloak seen from behind must cover the back and the legs down to its hem in every clip.

### Player-style art for every remaining NPC, outposts and all, with more distinct looks (owner request, 2026-11-11)
Owner: "replace all npcs. not just the big cities. make more". **Recounted 2026-11-12:** 588 actual people across the top-level Greenmeadow list and `zones[*].npcs`: 319 already assigned, **269 missing people**. Another 145 unassigned entries are mailboxes, meeting stones or scenery, excluded from people. The explicit plan (`docs/npc-named-plan.txt`) classifies 148 for bespoke art (134 quest givers plus 14 individual residents) and 121 for existing-role reuse. Grill answers (2026-11-11): (1) **Mix**: generic roles (traders, smiths, carters, Spark masters, guards, reeves, keepers) reuse existing designs with a recolour; named quest givers and people with a trait get NEW designs. (2) **"Make more" = more distinct looks**, a bigger design library so fewer people look alike; NO new NPCs in the world (no `map.txt` change, no server release). (3) Extra NPCs in towns/outposts: **undecided, ask again after the replacement**. (4) **One client release** (choose the next version from the live label), no restart or backup (client only); bump `client/index.html`, `scripts/sv-keep.txt`, `build_swedish.py`, newest entry in `client/release-notes.txt`.

### The opening cutscene for a new character (owner request, 2026-11-10)

- [x] `INTRO-RULES` — Owner: "make a cutscene when you've created a new character. it should be about a minute long. seeing the hero on a moving boat. the boat that is in greenmeadow. the camera zooming and panning around while the boat is moving towards its destination. the narrator should tell the story why the hero is summoned to this world. to save it from eldmarr. make up a one minute story of why the hero was summoned". **Decided (grill, 2026-11-10, two rounds):** (a) **Narrator: subtitles plus the browser's speech synthesis** (English or Swedish by the language setting; no voice available = subtitles only; follows the sound setting). (b) **Skipping: only from the second character of an account** (the first one must watch it; hold a key or press to skip; the server tells the client with `skippable`). (c) **Who and replay: new characters only (the `landing` flag at creation), saved as seen (`Character.cutscenes` kind `intro`), plus a Settings button "Replay intro"** (client side, always skippable). (d) **Tone: epic and solemn**, about 150 words from the approved Eldmarr history (`docs/molten-raid.md`: the first forge, the roots of the world tree, the broken promise of a seat at the gods' table, the nine chains, the fire rising for a hundred years). (e) **History visuals: the ship keeps sailing and the sky tells it**: ember-red horizon glow, drifting sparks, nine pale chain-like lightning strokes, a fire ring over a distant mountain, the water warming from blue to red and back; no painted tableaus. (f) **The summoning: the Grey Gull is already sailing; at "it found you" a ring of light opens on the deck and the hero appears in it** (real look and class); Skipper Halvard stands at the steering oar throughout and turns to look. (g) **Audio: synthesised ambience only** (waves, wind in the sail, creaks, a low ember rumble under the history, a soft rising chord at the summoning), through the sound setting. **My defaults (not asked):** the ship, pier and skipper are the game's own drawing (`meadowdock.js`, `City.drawNpc`) on a custom moving camera in the same 2:1 projection; the ship sails in along the pier and the last shot matches the game's first view of the dock, so the fade into the world is seamless; the server gives the hero the no-aggro grace for the whole intro and plays it once per character; test servers play it only after `enable_cutscenes`. Chain: RULES, SERVER, CLIENT, CHECK, RELEASE (with the crossing cutscenes, Beta 1.42.0).
- [x] `INTRO-SERVER` — **Done 2026-11-10 (committed, not released).** `join_as` queues `intro_pending`, `crossing_scenes` sends `intro` on the first tick (`landing` still set, unseen, not a test server unless opted in) with `skippable` from the account's character count, `INTRO_SECONDS` 62 + grace; `cargo test cutscene` 12 pass. After RULES: kind `intro` on a brand-new character (`landing` set, first tick), `skippable` from the account's other characters, 65 s grace.
- [x] `INTRO-CLIENT` — **Done.** `client/intro.js` (ship sailing in on a custom camera via `MeadowDock.ship/pier/moor`, the story in the sky, the summoning, the walk onto the pier, seamless fade), engine extensions in `cutscene.js` (`register`, narrator `speak`, hold-to-skip, per-scene fades, `pose.abs/walk/hidden`), `CutsceneAudio` ambience, Settings > Replay intro, Swedish by hand. After SERVER: `client/intro.js` scene, narrator and subtitles, skip, Settings replay, Swedish by hand.

Pictures from the real client were checked by eye (stills across the minute). Not done: a real new-character run on the public server, a Swedish voice check, a GIF for the owner (offer). After CLIENT: bounded browser test, pictures and a GIF for the owner.

### Cutscenes for the updraft and the dive (owner request, 2026-11-10)

- [x] `CUTSCENE-RULES` — Owner: "make a cutscene the first time flying up to bifrost. like the player getting carried away by the updraft and going super fast. the same when going down from Bifrost. and a cutscene the first time diving into the water. putting on the lead shoes and splashing into the water then sinking to the bottom. also the same the other way around. removing the lead shoes and floating towards the surface." **Decided (grill, 2026-11-10):** (a) **First time = per character, saved on the server** (new `Character` field, four kinds `sky_up`, `sky_down`, `dive`, `surface`; one-way migration; needs a server release). (b) **Locked, not skippable**: movement, skills and menus locked while it plays (client only), keep each scene about 6 s. (c) **Style: the hero stays centred and the world rushes past** (clouds, wind streaks, bubbles; the hero drawn with the real class art). (d) **Extras: synthesised sounds (WebAudio through `sfxOut`), captions (English and Swedish, written by hand), camera shake and flash.** Reduced motion keeps the animation and drops only shake and flash (the extras option text the owner picked; "still pictures" was not chosen). **My defaults (not asked):** zone kinds come from the server (`sky` theme = Bifrost, `Map::underwater()` for water, the same list as the lead shoes, so the cutscene and the shoes agree); Bifrost into the sea plays `sky_down` then `dive` as one sequence (and the sea into Bifrost `surface` then `sky_up`), each only if unseen, so both flags are set; the scene plays only on a real crossing (never on login, reconnect, Spark landings or respawns); mercenaries and ambient adventurers get nothing; the lead-shoes announce is dropped when the scene carries the same words; a timed no-aggro/no-damage grace like Spark's airborne protects the locked hero; a test-only debug op marks every cutscene seen so older UI tests are not locked. Chain: RULES, SERVER, CLIENT, CHECK, RELEASE (Beta 1.42.0, full server release).

### Aggro range follows the level gap (owner request, 2026-11-10)

- [x] `AGGRO-LEVEL` — **Done and released 2026-11-10 16:25 (Beta 1.40.3, `01f82fe`; `aggro_radius` in `world.rs`, `docs/aggro-level.md`, `cargo test aggro_level` 2 pass). Not done: the elite-pocket audit (elites above a hero now reach up to 2x the base radius that `design()` clears by +3 tiles).** Owner: "fix that monsters higher level than you has higher aggro range. and lower level have shorter aggro range." **Decided (grill, 2026-11-10):** (a) **Strength:** +-12% per level of difference, capped x2.0 above and x0.35 below (a monster 8 levels above you notices you from double the distance; caps reached at +-9 and about -5.4 levels). (b) **Scope:** every open-world monster including elites; the elite pockets must be audited against roads, places, seams and people (a higher-level elite now reaches further, e.g. Cairnspine's Behemoth 12.6 tiles from the Frostwake seam). Dungeons, raids, instances and bosses in them are NOT changed. (c) **Floor:** minimum 3 tiles, never fully passive. (d) **Whose level:** each player's own level against the monster's level (the nearest-player scan in `World::step_slime`, `server/src/world.rs` near `awareness`); an already chasing monster (`was_chasing` / `s.target`) is unchanged, no leash scaling. Docs: add the rule to the combat docs, update `docs/` elite-placement notes and release notes (`New/Changed`). Test only the new thing.

### Zone shapes follow the world-map provinces (owner request, 2026-11-05)

- [x] `MINIMAP-INTERIOR` - **Decided 2026-11-10 (owner): inside a dungeon, raid, instance, Arena or city interior the minimap shows that place's own layout, as it does now. The earlier idea (world map centred on the entrance) is dropped; nothing to build.** Still open as a minor note: the Sunward-Yearwheel doorway changes the minimap scale (different tile scale), and a zone build costs 0.3-1.4 s of main thread in the throttled test (about 10 per walk across the Stormglass region, one per 250 ms, cached).

**Owner request:** change the zones so they look like the zones on the world map, shape-wise (today every zone is a square grid; the painted atlas gives each province an irregular outline, `world/atlas-geography.txt`, 23 regions).

**Owner answers (grill, 2026-11-05):**
- **Scope: everything on the atlas.** The atlas has 23 polygon regions (zones 0-4, 6-9, 13-16, 19-23, 25-28 and Emberwake 45), cities included (Skaldholm, Nacrehold, Veyrroot Canopy). Asterion (17) is only a landmark point and Heartwood (24) a nested interior: no outline, open question. Dungeons, instances and raids are not provinces and keep their layout. (Corrected 2026-11-05: the first entry said 29 zones.)
- **Method: real relative scale.** Each zone is sized in proportion to its atlas area and takes the atlas outline (not a polygon carved into the current square). Distances, pacing, kill-time and travel balance shift with the new sizes, so expect re-checks of the affected zones only.
- **Edge: match the neighbour.** Where two provinces share a border on the atlas, that edge is the walking route/portal to the neighbour (portals tied to the shared borders, `Portal.walk` stays authoritative). Only true coastline and outer edges get themed scenery.
- **Rollout: preview first, then decide.** No map edits until the owner approves overlays. Do not start `SHAPE-DATA` before the owner says go.
- **Scale rule chosen (2026-11-05): `nofloor`.** Median k = 0.561 tiles per atlas px, but no zone's outline area drops below its current area and a city footprint must fit inside its outline (`min_scale_to_fit`: Skaldholm 1.33, Nacrehold 1.23). Resulting bounding boxes: Greenmeadow 236x135, Crags 203x195, Rimeveil 186x141, Gloamfen 159x185, Skaldholm 191x220, Wyrdwood 338x252, Bifrost 191x209, Ran's Deep 200x186, Nacrehold 301x264, Astralhollow 143x188, Prismwaste 163x156, Orrery 176x190, Moonspore 199x205, Stormglass 152x184, Sunward 173x194, Ribbonwild 205x164, Tanglecrown 240x220, Veyrroot Canopy 197x141, Lapis Erg 233x182, Frostwake 185x217, Cairnspine 188x251, Yearwheel 395x568, Emberwake 225x217 (`docs/zone-shapes-preview.md`; FileDrop `valhallasc-shape-z*-nofloor.png` for every zone). This is an owner-accepted deviation from strict proportion.
- **Asterion (17): keep as is** (owner, 2026-11-05); Heartwood (24) is a nested interior and keeps its layout too (default, not asked).
- **Not yet answered, proposed defaults apply until the owner says otherwise:** square engine grid (`size = max(W, H)`) with a masked `outline`; smooth each shared chain once so neighbours match.

**Defaults (not owner answers):** Work in a private worktree under `/media/raid/valhallasc-data`, never `.tools/`. The map is embedded in the server, so a shaped zone needs a rebuild and restart: nobody online, backup first, and saved characters standing in newly blocked cells (and private-copy IDs, `COPY` in `vault_tests.rs`) must be checked and moved. Generators are seeded and byte-stable; later generators in the chain preserve earlier zones, so decide per zone where the shaping step runs (`sync-world` runs last). Bump the version per release and add release notes.

**Merged into main and released 2026-11-10 (Beta 1.40.0, merge `c04a8e6`, server restarted 09:29, 37 saved characters migrated, backup `deploy/backups/zone-shapes-release-20261110-092623/`).** The per-zone entries below stay as history. Open after the release: first real login on the migrated characters, `MINIMAP-WORLD`, `SKALDHOLM-REBUILD` follow-ups, the pilot server `valhalla-shapes` (still the pre-merge binary) and its worktree (uncommitted `client/*art.js` edits from another session). Wiki `10_projects:valhallasc_zone_shapes_merged_and_released_20261110`.

- [x] `SHAPE-RULES` - Done 2026-11-05: `docs/zone-shapes.md` (proposal, pending owner approval): scope, scale rules (`median` proposed default, `total`, `nofloor` as a labelled deviation), outline-as-collision (new `outline` field + cell mask instead of thousands of `void` rectangles), shared borders as walking routes, per-chain smoothing, coast/land edge classes, handling of displaced content, process and four open owner questions.
- [x] `SHAPE-PREVIEW` - Done 2026-11-05, **awaiting owner approval**: `scripts/zone_shapes_preview.py` (read-only), `docs/zone-shapes-preview.md`, 30 FileDrop files `valhallasc-shape-*` (three overviews, 23 per-zone `-median` pictures, Skaldholm/Nacrehold/Yearwheel `-nofloor`, `valhallasc-shape-table.md`). **Findings:** strict proportion cannot hold Skaldholm's (126x126) or Nacrehold's (176x176) city at true size (need k 1.33/1.23 vs median 0.561); the Yearwheel shrinks to a tenth of its area (400x400 -> ~123x177, with 8 portals and the Black Hour door); Greenmeadow/Crags grow 2.3x/3.0x in area; 169 of 972 spawns fall outside under stretch mapping; 12 of 94 border portals are more than 25 tiles from their shared border. **Owner decisions needed before `SHAPE-DATA`:** scale rule (`median` strict, `nofloor` with cities/Yearwheel exempt, or `total`), Asterion/Heartwood, square engine grid with a masked outline vs rectangular grid, smoothing.
- [x] `MINIMAP-WORLD` - **DONE 2026-11-10, Beta 1.41.0 (client only, live on save): `drawMini` in `client/field.js` draws every shaped k = 1 zone whose box reaches the view at its atlas offset (`shape.T` difference; 70 of 72 seam/vault shifts already equal it), water first then land, fog and NPC/Spark/quest marks per zone with no fade at borders; canvases are cached per zone (8 soft, 14 hard) and built one per timer tick; `tests/minimap-ui.cjs` (23 checks, `FIELD_JS=<old field.js>` must fail on the Stormglass-Frostwake gate pixel). NOT done: dungeons, raids, Arena, Asterion, Moonwell, Heartwood and the Yearwheel (own scale 2.8) keep their own old minimap, not the entrance-centred world view; the Sunward-Yearwheel doorway is the one crossing where the minimap still changes. Owner question open: should interiors show the world map centred on the entrance with a 'you are inside' marker?** **Go (owner, 2026-11-10, grilled): the wait is over; every shaped outdoor province plus Skaldholm and Heartwood joins, dungeons/instances/Asterion/Moonwell/city interiors: the owner's earlier answer (11-06) was the world map centred on the entrance with an 'inside' marker; first release leaves them on their own minimap, to confirm. Answers: (1) background = each zone's REAL ground rendered as now and stitched into the atlas frame in chunks, not the painted atlas; (2) zoom steps unchanged (zoom 1 = 240-tile span, up to 4x closer), the only change is that the background never changes at a border; (3) built straight in the live checkout (owner's choice, client is live on save; tested through a scratch copy served by `FIELD_JS`, saved once; a feature, so Beta 1.41.0).** Earlier note: waited until every zone was reshaped (owner confirmed, 2026-11-06). Stopgap on the pilot: every shaped zone shares one plain minimap background (no sea fill or ripples; coast keeps its shallows and foam). Owner: "i want the minimap to be the whole world. like the zones are, and never change when crossing a border." Today it layers this zone plus its seam neighbours, so the background changes at a crossing (blue sliver between Crags and meadow seen from the meadow, black from the Crags). Plan: one world map in the atlas frame (1 tile = 1 atlas px, a zone's position is `shape.T`), centred on the hero, so a crossing swaps nothing; build it in chunks (2048x1041 at the current 2.4 px per tile would be about 49 MB as one canvas). **Owner answers:** fog over all unexplored land in every province, sea stays visible, same as the world map's island fog; inside places not on the atlas (dungeons, raids, Arena, city interiors) keep showing the world map centred on the entrance, with a marker that you are inside; NPCs, travel masters and quest markers from every zone in view with no fade at borders, enemies limited to what the server sends near you. Unshaped provinces: no fallback art needed if this waits.

### Frame size per kind (owner request, 2026-11-04)

**Owner requirement:** every enemy kind may have **its own sprite frame size and anchor**, not just one size per zone manifest, so a boss can be large and a critter small inside the same zone (including the shared Cathedral manifest). This is the prerequisite for using the "frame size is free" rule below at kind level; until it is done, a zone picks one frame size for all its kinds.

**Done (2026-11-04), staged and uncommitted pending owner approval (client files are already live on disk, behaviour-neutral, no version bump):** the whole chain is implemented and checked; contract in `docs/kind-frames.md`. The owner's four answers are what it implements; the remakes may now start (owner order: Greenmeadow, Rimeveil, Gloamfen, Undervault, Wyrdwood, Bifrost).

**Defaults (not owner answers):** Fully backwards compatible: a manifest without per-kind entries behaves exactly as today, every live atlas and manifest stays byte-identical, and no zone's art, stats, hitboxes or IDs change until its own remake task runs. Server untouched (art only). Mechanics found in the code: the client reads one `meta.frame` and `meta.anchor` per zone manifest in `field.js` (`src.meta.frame/anchor` in the portrait code near line 2930 and the draw code near line 2956, plus about 20 per-zone loaders that build `{meta, img}` and `SLIME[k].top` from `meta.tops`), `emptyseat.js` and `magesprite.js`/`npc-art.js` for other sprite families (leave those alone). Proposed manifest shape: an optional `kindFrames: {kind: {frame: [w, h], anchor: [x, y]}}`; a kind absent from it uses the manifest `frame`/`anchor`. An atlas is still 8 columns by 5 rows of that kind's own frame size (34 frames, five clips, same row order).

**Decided by the owner (2026-11-04, grill round 1):** (a) **Scale: same art pixel scale.** One art pixel stays the same size on screen, so a larger frame draws a visibly larger creature and a smaller frame a smaller one (frame size is real size; no per-kind draw-scale field, no fixed on-screen footprint). (b) **Collision: art and click hitbox only.** Server collision, aggro, attack ranges, pathing and balance stay exactly as they are; only the drawn size, the client click/target hitbox, shadow and health-bar position follow the frame (no Rust change, no TTK/boss recalibration). (c) **Maximum frame: 192 x 192** (a 34-frame atlas is 1536x960, about 6 MB of texture per kind); `KINDFRAME-RULES` records it and `KINDFRAME-EXPORT` refuses larger frames (minimum stays sensible: the exporter needs room for the border check). (d) **Order: this chain first, then the remakes.** Do `KINDFRAME-RULES` to `KINDFRAME-DOCS`, then redo the six reopened zones (Greenmeadow, Rimeveil, Gloamfen, Undervault, Wyrdwood, Bifrost) with per-kind sizes available; do not start another zone's remake before the chain is checked.

- [x] `KINDFRAME-RULES` — Write the contract in `docs/` (manifest shape above or a better one the code shows is simpler; the fallback rule; atlas layout per kind; anchor must keep planted feet on the ground row; how `tops`, hitbox, shadow, portrait crop and nameplate/health-bar placement derive from the kind's own frame; limits such as a maximum frame size for texture memory). List every reader of `meta.frame`/`meta.anchor` for enemy art and decide which need the per-kind lookup. No code yet.
- [x] `KINDFRAME-CLIENT` — Add one lookup helper (`frameOf(src, kind)` returning `{w, h, ax, ay}`) and use it in every enemy-art reader in `field.js` (portrait, draw, hitbox, health-bar `top`, shadow, culling/bounds, boss and helper art, the Cathedral and per-zone loaders), keeping the zone-wide values as the fallback. Never allocate per frame: cache per kind. Do not touch NPC, mount, class-sprite or chest paths.
- [x] `KINDFRAME-EXPORT` — Teach the shared sprite tooling (the `*_slime_redraw.py` exporter pattern, `scripts/make_*_sprites.py` helpers, PixelFlow `create` width/height per kind, border and bounds assertions, `tops` derivation, the rollback scripts) to build and export a kind at its own size and write `kindFrames` only for kinds that differ. The shared Cathedral manifest must merge per kind without clobbering other kinds' entries (see the Cloister exporter and `rollback_cloister_art.py`).
- [x] `KINDFRAME-CHECK` — Bounded check with a scratch manifest holding kinds at three sizes (for example 64x64, 96x96 and the 192x192 maximum) and that a 193 px frame is refused: correct draw position and anchor, hitbox, health-bar top, shadow and portrait for each, nothing clipped, 34 frames each. Prove backwards compatibility: every live zone's manifest and atlas hashes are unchanged, and a render of one kind per live zone is pixel-identical before and after the change. Mutation: reverting one reader to the zone-wide size must fail. No full suites; use `scripts/test-touch-bounded.sh`.
- [x] `KINDFRAME-DOCS` — Update the sprite docs, the `createmap`/`makesprites` skills' notes and the remake tasks below (replace "one frame size per zone" with the per-kind rule); record the release in the wiki. Deployment only after the owner approves.

**Death animation rule:** The death clip must suit the creature's material and anatomy, not a generic collapse. Examples: slimes and jellies collapse or burst; fire gutters out or flares and dies down; ghosts and spirits dissipate or fade upward; undead and skeletons fall apart; stone, metal and armour crack and slump in pieces; insects and arthropods curl up or flip over; animals and beasts fall over; plants wither, wilt or splinter; machines seize, spark and stop; sea creatures sink or deflate; bosses get a longer, more dramatic version of their own kind. Judge each kind individually; never reuse one death for unlike creatures. The **Crags** candidate follows this already (fire extinguishes, ghost dissipates, spider and golem buckle or slump): its CHECK must also confirm that its five death clips are distinct and fit. The **Greenmeadow slimes** keep their collapse.

**Same corrections as the Crags:** Do not use squash/collapse where it does not make sense. Keep the slime's shading, eyes and proportions with movement appropriate to each material (rigid shells, stone and armour step, strike and slump; fire deforms and extinguishes; ghosts drift and dissipate).

### Remake every NPC in the player-character style, keeping their distinctive traits (owner request, 2026-11-03)

**Owner requirement:** Remake **all NPCs** in the same art style as the **player characters** (the cel-shaded class/body sprites: same proportions, shading, eyes and animation feel), but **keep each NPC's distinctive traits** (silhouette, build, hair, headwear, clothing colours, props and region materials) so every person stays recognisable.

**Defaults (not owner answers):** Artwork only: no names, routes, offers, dialogue, quests, interaction distances, saved data, stats or zone IDs change. Same 96x96 frame, foot anchor (48,90), idle 6 / walk 8 / talk 6 frames and the same NPC art IDs and assignments (`client/assets/npc.txt`, 164 designs, 319 identities), so no server rebuild. Build in a private worktree under `/media/raid/valhallasc-data`, new PixelFlow sprites under a new name prefix (old PixelFlow IDs stay saved), old atlases and manifest kept outside the live tree for rollback. Work in batches by design group (the 24 general designs first, then the 140 special designs by region) and **stage each batch for the owner's approval before anything is deployed**. Keep prop grips attached to the animated wrist (`tests/npc-grips.py`), the head sockets that `NpcArt.head` reads for seasonal hats, ears and wreaths, and the foot/nameplate bounds. Stones and speaking scenery keep their scenery artwork. Per `docs/npc-art.md` and `scripts/npc_art_catalog.py` / `make_npc_sprites.py`; read `docs/character-bodies.md` for the player proportions. Not deployed until the owner has seen the pictures and approved.

**Decided by the owner (2026-11-03, grill round 1):** (a) **Proportions: exact player proportions.** Every human NPC uses the player's body and head ratio, so differences come from clothing, hair, headwear, props, palette and posture, not from a different build; this supersedes keeping the 24 deliberately different silhouettes (the "24 distinct silhouettes" check becomes "24 distinct looks": hair, headwear, clothing and prop). Children and elders may need an owner-checked sample because they have no player equivalent. (b) **Non-humans: same style, keep their anatomy**: player shading, eyes and animation feel, but fins, caps, rings, branches and brass stay. (c) **Order:** sample trio first for the owner's go/no-go, then the 24 general designs, then the 140 special designs region by region, with a picture batch each. (d) **Deploy per batch after the owner approves it**, so towns may temporarily mix old and new art.

### Seasonal NPC hair accessories and two-hour festival buff (owner request, 2026-11-03)

**Owner requirements:** Summer Solstice NPCs should have **flowers in their hair**; Valentine's NPCs should have **hearts**, and Easter NPCs should have **Easter eggs** as their seasonal hair/head accessories. **Every season** must offer a **two-hour buff granting +10% attack and +50% experience**. This applies to Halloween, Christmas, New Year, Valentine's, Easter and Summer Solstice, and must use the shared seasonal system so future seasons are covered too.

**Owner-proposed buff ritual:** Press/interact with the season's centerpiece, then dance **two complete rotations around it** to earn the buff. Summer Solstice uses the **midsummer pole**, Christmas a **Christmas tree**, and Valentine's a **heart**. Apply the same ritual to every other season with a fitting centerpiece (Easter egg, Halloween pumpkin and New Year star/rocket are proposed implementation defaults, not additional owner choices). Count two full circuits around the object, rather than two spins in place. This owner-proposed acquisition flow is implemented with the guided movement and interruption defaults recorded in the candidate's seasonal rules.

**Scope/status (2026-11-03):** All eight follow-up deliverables are implemented and privately verified as **Beta 1.38.0** in `/media/raid/valhallasc-data/solstice-work`, branch `feat/summer-solstice`. **Local/uncommitted; not pushed, merged to main or deployed.** All six seasonal candidates are integrated here; mailboxes remains separate. Rules: candidate `docs/seasonal-events.md`; evidence and all61 verified requested FileDrop pictures: `docs/festival-spirit-validation.md`, wiki `10_projects:valhallasc_seasonal_buff_hair_20261103`. Guided automatic two-lap dance,24 clear city centerpieces, saved non-stacking two-hour buff, offline countdown and refresh, exact attack/XP modifiers; flowers/hearts/decorated eggs fitted to103NPCs/3708animated poses. Seven new Rust tests,9 focused compatibility checks,9 MCP,35 browser-flow/25 real-art checks and targeted phone/HUD follow-ups pass; mutation is caught, generators are byte-stable and gates pass. Recorded defaults cover interruption, refresh/no stacking, offline/death/rebirth/season expiry and additive base XP. No new zones/schema/copy-ID migration, grinding, encounter timing/balance sweeps or live game changes.

### Summer Solstice season and Swedish Midsummer festivities (owner request, 2026-11-03)

**Owner requirements:** Add an annual season named **Summer Solstice**, with **Swedish Midsummer decorations**, including a **midsummer pole (midsommarstång)** and coordinated festive dressing. Add the regular **summer-solstice-themed seasonal quests**, plus **one five-player elite quest with an exact 5% chance to drop a solstice-themed mount**. The season starts **four days before the summer solstice** and ends **four days after**.

**Calendar interpretation/default:** Anchor the event to the **Northern Hemisphere's astronomical June solstice**, calculated for each year, and take the calendar date containing that instant in **Europe/Stockholm** as `S`. Start at Stockholm midnight on **`S − 4 calendar days`**; **`S + 4` is the last active date, inclusive**, with expiry at midnight on **`S + 5`** (nine active calendar dates). Use local calendar arithmetic. The astronomical solstice determines availability; Swedish Midsummer supplies the visual/cultural theme. Verify yearly solstice dates against an independent authoritative astronomical source during implementation, and document the calculation's supported year range and accuracy.

**Seasonal defaults:** Dress **Alderhaven, Skaldholm, Nacrehold and Asterion** within their actual city bounds. Follow the existing structure with **four annual city errands total, one per city, plus one shared daily elite quest offered/claimable in all four cities**. Default to **one independent 5% mount roll per eligible real player per Stockholm day**, with a persisted attempt ledger separate from quest claims; hires count toward the five fighters but never roll. Freeze the highest participating fighter level and participant IDs at summon. Mount/elite identity, summon landmark/location and quest IDs are to be defined in the rules, preserving the solstice theme and exact requested chance. These are implementation defaults following the other seasons, not additional owner answers.

Astronomical June-solstice/Stockholm nine-date window, four Midsummer cities, four annual errands/shared daily Birchcrown Guardian and exact personal 5% Sunwoven Stag roll. 11 new Rust plus 3 calendar compatibility checks, 10 MCP checks, 25 core UI assertions, 69 real-art checks/1,236 NPC poses and targeted Swedish-phone/table follow-ups pass; decoration mutation is caught and gates/full-sync stability pass. ### Easter season, city quests, bunny mount, achievement and title (owner request, 2026-11-03)

**Owner requirements:** Add an annual **Easter season**, calculating Easter Sunday with the **Meeus/Jones/Butcher algorithm**, then applying **−2 days for the start**, with the end **10 days after**. Add Easter decorations in **Alderhaven, Skaldholm, Nacrehold and Asterion**, the regular seasonal city quests, and **one five-player elite quest with an exact 5% chance to drop an Easter bunny mount**. Completing **all distinct Easter quests** awards an **achievement and an Easter-themed title**.

**Date interpretation:** Let `E` be Gregorian Easter Sunday for the current year. Start at Stockholm midnight on **`E − 2 days`**; the **last active date is `start + 10 calendar days` (`E + 8 days`), inclusive**, with expiry at the following midnight (`E + 9 days`). This records “10 days after” relative to the start date as the implementation assumption. Use local calendar-date arithmetic, including March/April rollover and Stockholm daylight-saving transitions; never a fixed month/day window or elapsed multiples of 24 hours.

**Chosen rewards/defaults:** Mount **Springtide Bunny**, a large rideable white rabbit with pastel-pink ears, a floral saddle and a small decorated egg basket. Achievement **Eggstraordinary Easter**, **25 points** to match the other seasonal quest-set achievements, unlocking permanent suffix title **`<name> the Springbringer`** (for example, **`Sork the Springbringer`**). Follow the existing seasonal structure: **four annual city quests total, one per city, plus one shared daily elite quest offered/claimable in all four cities**. Default to **one independent 5% mount roll per eligible real player per Stockholm day**, with a persisted roll ledger separate from quest claims; hires count toward the five fighters but never roll. One claimed elite quest counts toward the all-quests set; permanent completion history across years counts. These are implementation choices following existing seasons, not additional owner answers.

The four city/travel tasks are implemented, verified and deployed in Beta 1.33.0 (2026-11-02). The seven earlier reports below are implemented, verified and deployed in Beta 1.32.8 (2026-11-02).

### Christmas season, city quests, Grinch daily hunt and rewards (owner’s request, 2026-11-02)

**Owner requirements:** Add **Christmas every year from December 15 through December 28 inclusive**, using the existing authoritative **Europe/Stockholm** seasonal calendar (start December 15 midnight; end December 29 midnight). **Alderhaven, Skaldholm, Nacrehold and Asterion** should be **very festive and Christmassy**, with coordinated NPC dressing and rich decorations throughout their actual city bounds. Add **3–4 Christmas quests in the cities**, plus **one shared daily quest offered in all four cities** to defeat **the Grinch** in **Rimeveil Glacier (zone 2)**. A **big Christmas tree** appears there during Christmas; a player on the quest presses/interacts with the tree to summon the Grinch. The Grinch is a **five-player elite**, with difficulty scaled to the **highest-level player in the party**, rather than Halloween’s rounded average. The shared quest is **repeatable daily**. The Grinch has a **5% chance to drop a festive red-nosed reindeer mount**. Completing **all Christmas quests** awards an achievement and the suffix title **`<Name> the Merry`**.

Reuse the seasonal calendar, expiry rules, shared-city daily allowance, persistent reward ownership and achievement/title systems. Christmas scenery, NPC costumes, tree/summon and unfinished quests retire automatically at December 29 midnight; earned mounts, achievements and titles remain. The Rimeveil encounter tree is an explicitly requested seasonal landmark outside the four-city dressing scope. **Before implementation, settle the unspecified rules:** whether 3–4 city quests means the total across the cities or per city; city-quest cadence and same-year versus historical completion for the achievement; whether the 5% chance is one party drop or an independent roll per eligible player, and the daily attempt/duplicate-ownership rules. Record decisions without silently changing the requested chance. Existing Halloween hire eligibility is the starting convention; document Christmas eligibility and freeze the highest-party-level scaling at summon time.

**Implementation defaults (2026-11-02; optional owner clarification requested):** Follow Halloween with **four city errands total, one per city, once per year**; **one independent 5% reindeer roll per eligible real player per Stockholm day**, hires count toward the five fighters but never roll; both seasonal achievements accept authoritative completions **across years**, with one claimed daily hunt counting once. These are stated implementation assumptions, not new owner answers. Keep the highest of all five participating fighters’ levels fixed for the Grinch summon.

### Halloween summoned elite, daily city quest and rare mount (owner’s request, 2026-11-02)

Owner requirements: a **five-player Halloween special elite**, summoned in a chosen zone, that **scales to the party’s levels**. **All four cities offer the same quest, completable once per day.** The elite drops a **new mount at exactly 1% chance**. Proposed mount concept: a **nightmare steed with a burning mane**. Use the existing Halloween window, **October 26–November 9 inclusive**, on the authoritative Stockholm calendar.

**Chosen setting:** **Gloamfen (zone 3)**, at a dedicated, reachable ritual clearing away from existing elites, services, gates and ordinary through-paths. Proposed elite: **The Hollow Rider**, a headless revenant knight with a blazing jack-o’-lantern and spectral steed; summoned by lighting a haunted ritual lantern. Final coordinates, summon presentation and mechanics belong to the zone generator/design work below. The existing four annual city errands remain separate from this shared daily hunt.

**Owner decisions (2026-11-02):** Each eligible real player gets **one independent 1% mount roll per Stockholm calendar day**. The elite level is the **average of the five participating fighters’ levels, rounded up**, fixed when summoned. Existing duplicate names: **oldest character keeps the name; the others must choose a new name before entering**, preserving character IDs and all progress. Hired fighters count toward the five-person encounter; they do not receive mount rolls. Award mount ownership directly on a successful eligible kill so a full bag cannot lose the rare reward; subsequent kills that day cannot reroll. Daily quest completion and mount-roll ledgers are persisted separately. Completed/owned rewards persist after the season; at November 10 midnight costumes/props disappear, quests and summons stop, any remaining Rider despawns, and unfinished quests retire. These are implementation rules for the owner’s selected options.

Define who can summon, kill/contribution credit, occupied-clearing protection, reset/wipe/despawn behavior, daily reset at Stockholm midnight, and behavior at event end. One shared quest ID and daily completion allowance across Alderhaven, Skaldholm, Nacrehold and Asterion: changing cities must not grant extra daily completions. ### Swing and Cleave streaks drawn in front of the character (owner's report, 2026-11-01)

- [x] `SKILL-FX-LAYERING` — **Done and deployed, Beta 1.32.8 (2026-11-02).** Proof: `docs/todo-fixes-validation.md`. Original report: Owner examples: Cleave and the regular swing. When swinging upwards (away from the camera) the blue streak is drawn **in front of** the character, which looks wrong: the blade is behind the body at that moment. Causes found in `client/field.js`: the basic `slash`/`dualSlash` arc (`#59d9ff`/`#bff6ff`, around line 4032) is drawn in one pass "over everything", and `skCleave`'s moving steel edge is deliberately in the `air` layer after actors (its sector is on the floor). Fix: depth-sort the swing by direction, so the part of the arc on the far side of the hero (facing up/away from the camera) is drawn before the body and the near side after it, split by the hero's screen y, and do the same for the Cleave edge and other melee arcs (`skAssassinFan`, `skCone`, Assassin `dualSlash`); also check remote players and monsters' swings. Keep it consistent with the sprite weapon swing (the `heroSpr` path currently skips the slash: check whether the sprite already draws its own streak). Keep the cached ground and render-cost rules. Then audit the remaining `sk*` effects and the Mage/Priest/Hunter buff shells for the same far-side-over-body problem. Update `docs/combat-feedback.md`. Test only this: a bounded browser check that, for an upward swing and an upward Cleave, hero body pixels are not covered by the streak while a downward swing still is drawn in front, with a mutation that restores the single pass and must fail.

### Zone gates are hard to hit (owner's report, 2026-11-01)

- [x] `PORTAL-GATE-REACH` — **Done and deployed, Beta 1.32.8 (2026-11-02).** Proof: `docs/todo-fixes-validation.md`. Original report: Changing zones is finicky: the spot you must reach to trigger a gate is too small and easily missed. Cause to confirm: `use_portal` (`world.rs`) fires only when the hero is within `portal.r` of the gate centre, and most radii in `world/map.txt` are small (74 gates at 1.2, 27 at 1.1, 52 at 2.0 tiles), and walk-through gates (`walk` side) also need the hero to be moving toward the exit, so clicking or tapping near the edge, stopping short, or arriving from the side does nothing. Fix on the player's side without opening new exploits: make the trigger area larger and more forgiving (for example a wider trigger zone across the whole gate opening and/or a higher minimum radius), let a click or tap on or near a gate route the hero through it (`routeTo`/`pullRoute` in `client/field.js`, touch included), and show where the gate is (visible marker or glow on the edge, hover cursor). Keep arrivals outside every gate disc with no enemy within 8 units (existing Rust test), do not make arrival spots re-trigger the gate (the `PORTAL_DELAY` guard), and keep the access rules (`needs_quest`, `needs_boss`, `min_level`, sealed templates). Re-check the world-map atlas walking routes (`Portal.walk`, `compact_atlas`) and Bifrost arrivals still pass. Test only this: one focused Rust check that a hero just outside the old radius now passes, and one bounded browser check for click and touch near a gate, mouse and phone.

### Phone and tablet play (owner's request, 2026-10-24)

**Built and checked, 2026-10-25 (Beta 1.17.0):** the owner approved `docs/touch-play.md` on 2026-10-24 and has not yet tested the Safari zoom fix. Six build tasks below are complete; real-device acceptance remains open. The uncapped audit exhausted host RAM and was stopped after the owner rebooted. A tablet ground-cache defect (33–35 visible chunks versus 31 retained) is fixed; stationary drawing now reuses its canvases. Browser runs are capped by `scripts/test-touch-bounded.sh` at 1.5 GiB for the entire process tree, no swap, one CPU and ten minutes; uncapped runs are refused. The final indexed combined check passed 198 assertions at both target sizes, with a 1214 MiB peak; seven mutations fail for their intended assertions. No uncapped retries or system package installation.

Owner: "Make the game more playable on a phone or tablet." Decided in a grill round (2026-10-24). **Starting point (2026-10-24):** `viewport-fit=cover` is set, `body` has `touch-action: manipulation`, `#fieldcv` has `touch-action: none`, tap on the ground routes the hero, `@media (pointer: coarse)` already gives many controls 44 px floors (portrait menu, windows, bank, shop), and `style.css` shows a `.rotate` overlay in portrait under 700 px. There is no joystick, no touch skill layout, no fullscreen or manifest, and no Low graphics setting. **Decided:** (1) **Movement:** a floating on-screen joystick on the left thumb plus the existing tap-to-move (tapping the ground still routes there); shown only on touch devices (`pointer: coarse`), never on a mouse desktop, and it must feed the same movement path as held keys so the server rules (80 messages/s, idle every 250 ms, moving every 50 ms) are unchanged. (2) **Orientation: landscape only.** Keep the rotate overlay in portrait and make landscape good on phones (about 844x390) and tablets (about 1024x768); no portrait layout. (3) **Skills: the player chooses.** Big thumb buttons in an arc at the bottom right (an attack button and a few skill buttons); the player assigns which of the 12 skills sit on them, reusing the Edit UI layout mode (`Prefs.layout`, `ITEMS`); the other slots stay in the bar or a tray; potions/food (`#quickuse`) and target actions need a reachable touch place too. Autoattack behaviour is unchanged (`docs/autoattack.md`). (4) **Scope, all four chosen:** installable and fullscreen (fullscreen button, `.webmanifest` since Apache blocks `.json`, add-to-home-screen icon, no pull-to-refresh or pinch zoom on the canvas, safe-area insets for notches); every window fits and scrolls at 844x390 (bags, character, bank, shop, quests, world map, trade, inspect, skills, settings, raid windows; mind `WINDOWS` scale caps and `--ui`); a **Low graphics** setting in `Settings` that is suggested on first touch visit (fewer effects, capped frame rate, lower canvas scale; render-cost lessons in `../AGENTS.md`); a test pass at 844x390 and 1024x768 with touch emulation (a Playwright **context** with `isMobile`/`hasTouch` and a coarse pointer). **Rules for the builder:** new keys or buttons that act go through `Keys`/`ACTIONS` where a key exists (`client/keymap.js`); touch buttons need `pointer-events: auto` and 44 px width AND height floors; hand-written Swedish for every new string (never a translator), `translate="no"` for nothing new; read-test `Prefs` migration (old saved layouts drop unknown keys); new HUD pieces go in `ITEMS`/`EXTRA`, never nested in another piece; check what the `.rotate` overlay hides. **Open for the builder / owner:** joystick dead zone, size and opacity, and whether it also appears on a touch laptop (propose, ask only if it matters); which default skills sit on the arc per class. Version bump per the rules (middle number for the joystick/skill layout release, last number for later fixes).

Decisions from two grill rounds (2026-10-24): (1) **When:** once per new version, the first time a player opens the game after the version number changed; remembered per browser (localStorage, own namespace, never shared with Valhalla). (2) **New players:** a first-ever visit gets a short welcome instead of release notes (controls plus goal: what the game is, how to move and fight, where to go first, Greenmeadow; key names read from the current `Keys` bindings, never hard-coded letters); then the current version is marked seen. (3) **Content:** one plain notes file in the client (one entry per version: New / Changed / Removed lists, simple everyday words, no code or file names, a removal always says why). May list new things, changes and fixes, removed things, and balance changes in plain words with the nearest whole percentage (owner, 2026-10-24: "not exact numbers. but maybe the nearest percentage"), e.g. "dungeon monsters hit about 30% softer"; never multipliers, decimals or constants like x0.7. **English only: no Swedish for this window (owner, 2026-10-24), so the notes text and the welcome stay out of `sv-*.txt`; the window's own fixed labels (title, Close, section headings) also stay untranslated and the screen must not fail the Swedish catalog check (add the strings to `scripts/sv-keep.txt` or `sv-ignore.txt` as that check requires).** (4) **Look:** a centred window like the game's other windows, version number as the title, headed lists, one Close button (Close, Esc and the Enter key all close it); it must be in `WINDOWS` in `settings.js` so UI size and window scale apply, and sit above z-index 49; no "don't show again" box. (5) **Reopening:** a "What's new" row in Settings reopens the latest notes, and the window has an "Older updates" section to browse earlier versions. (6) **History:** write up every version since the 1.14.32 baseline (selling window, green vendor weapons, mercenary wall following, login timer, autoattack fix, Ember Key and the raid, easier dungeons and arena, death and repair, and so on, each in plain language, written from `git log` and the TODO entries), then keep it going. (7) **Process:** every version bump (see "Version bumps for every game change" in AGENTS.md) must add its entry in the same change; a test fails when the version in `client/index.html` has no entry in the notes file. Needs a version bump (new feature, so the middle number: `x.N.x`), a real-client check (`MUTATE=1` must fail): first visit shows the welcome, a new version shows its notes once and not again after Close and reload, Settings reopens it, history lists older versions, Esc closes it, no text in the window uses a code word. Client only, no server restart. Wiki `10_projects:valhallasc_whats_new_<date>`.

### Bugs (owner's reports, 2026-10-21)

- [x] `BUG-WALK-TO-STUTTER` — **Done 2026-10-21 (client only, live on disk, no restart).** Reproduced in a real Chromium (Visit Alderhaven from (80,25), server positions from the snapshots): the hero moved 1.4 tiles/s instead of 5.2, one tick of walking and then 100-500 ms still, at every waypoint. Cause: `routeTo` returned a waypoint on every tile, and the follower (`update()` in `field.js`) advanced only when the **drawn** hero was within .32 of it; the drawn hero eases toward the server position and trails it by about speed/14 = .37 tiles, so the server reached each waypoint (`Move` goal cleared at .12), stood idle, and waited for the sprite to catch up before the next `move` went out. Fix: (1) `pullRoute` keeps only the waypoints a straight, clear walk cannot skip (the grid cells' .55 clearance; `segmentFree` is now shared and takes a margin), so the 98-tile Alderhaven route is 11 waypoints; (2) the follower judges progress by the server position (`hero.nx/ny`) and sends the next waypoint speed x .45 s early (`routeMotion.v`, the measured speed), only when the straight line to it clears objects at .45; the last waypoint ends at .32 as before. Result: every snapshot shows the full 5.2 tiles/s, with at most an occasional one-tick hiccup from a slow frame. Check `tests/walk-route-ui.cjs` (8 checks, reads speed from the snapshots' game time; mutation-checked by serving the committed `field.js`: median 2.6 tiles/s fails). Original task: Pathing: when the player presses "walk to Alderhaven" (or any other walk-to / route destination), the character stutters forward instead of walking smoothly. Reproduce first in a real client (headless Chromium with a private server): walk to a hub from the world map or the journal, log the hero's position every frame and the server's snapshots and look for the gaps (a goal that is re-sent or re-planned every tick, a client correction that snaps back against the server position, route waypoints that are reached and re-chosen, input and goal fighting each other: `ClientMessage::Input` clears the goal). Fix the cause, then add a check that the hero's frame-to-frame step stays even while following a route (assert the largest step against the median, not an attribute).

Decide with the owner which messages count as important (start with the treasure chest spawn and despawn, `CHEST-ANNOUNCE`, and the arena gauntlet fight start and result; later boss spawns, elite spawns, rare drops, level up and zone events), where the server sends them (a new `announce` packet with a text and a kind, so the server decides what is important), and whether they also go to the chat log.
