### Arena gauntlet quests (owner's request, 2026-10-21)

At level 20 and every ten levels after (20, 30, ... 90 and 99), the player gets a quest for the Arena (zone 40). The quest teleports the player to the Arena, where they face mercenaries one at a time, one of each class (warrior, mage, assassin, priest, hunter), so five fights. When a fight ends the player is restored to full health and mana, and a countdown runs before the next match. Winning all five completes the quest. Turning it in pays a blue weapon for that level, experience and gold.

- [x] `ARENA-Q-RULES` — **Done 2026-10-21; merged and released.** Write `docs/arena-quests.md` from the owner's decisions (2026-10-21) and settle what is still open. **Decided:** quests at levels 20, 30, ... 90 and 99 (nine); a giver in each safe hub (Alderhaven, Skaldholm, Nacrehold, Asterion; any hub's giver offers it, the quest id is the same); five fights, one opponent per class, random order each run; opponents are at the quest's level in ordinary green gear (not the player's level, not scaled to the player's gear); **PvP rules apply** (`pvp_hit`, `PVP_DAMAGE_SCALE` 0.5, stun/root/wound, ward, priest heal scale, `pvp_fight` lifesteal block), so the opponents are driven through the same code as a duel; 10 s countdown between matches; full health and mana after each win; a loss resets the whole run with no XP loss and no cost, and the player retries by talking to a giver again; solo only (no party members or other players), potions and food allowed with their usual shared timers; once per level per character, and the blue weapon is a one-time reward; reward = blue weapon of the player's class at the quest level, `rewardXp` = levels.txt[level-1]/10, and gold by the usual quest formula. **Decided (round 3, 2026-10-21):** no win-rate target: build it with plain green-gear opponents at the quest's level and report the measured win rate per class and level in `docs/arena-quests.md` (the owner reacts to the numbers; do not tune PvP constants); gold = the existing gold of a hard quest at that level; opponents are named after their class ('Arena Warrior' and so on), drawn with the ordinary class sprites and green gear, with a nameplate that marks them as opponents, and their name and any text need hand-written Swedish; a fight ends at 1 health like a duel or arena match (nobody dies, a defeat only resets the run, no death penalty, no corpse run).
**Decided (answers to the open questions, 2026-10-21):** (1) the quest is offered from its level upward and never expires, so a level-35 player can still take the level-30 quest; the reward is the weapon of the quest's level, and each quest is done once per character. (2) Leaving the Arena mid-run (disconnect, logout, `ArenaLeave`, portal) resets the run and uses the Arena's existing restore (`c.zone` is an arena zone: the character is placed back outside, see the load check near world.rs:1920), nothing is paid or lost. (3) Each fight has a cap of 180 s (tune it from the duel harness's typical fight length); reaching it counts as a loss and resets the run, since a draw cannot pass a fight. (4) Opponents are real `Player`s with `merc: None` and a new `gauntlet: Some(owner)` marker (not `merc` and not `ambient`: `pvp_legal` in pvp.rs returns false for both, so a hired-style opponent could never hurt or be hurt), driven by `ai_combat_at` exactly like the duel harness; `pvp_legal` gets one new case: the owner and his own gauntlet opponent in the same Arena copy. They are never saved, never count as humans, take no connection slot, and fall under the ambient/merc exclusions for who, invites, party and loot. (5) Every run gets its own private Arena copy, from the same pool as queue matches (`make_instances`: eight playable copies, `!i.active` is the free test); when none is free the giver answers that the Arena is full and the quest stays as it was.
- [x] `ARENA-Q-SERVER` — **Done 2026-10-21; merged and released.** New objective kind (for example `gauntlet`) in the quest catalog; a per-player Arena run in Rust, in its own private Arena copy and with the `gauntlet` opponent marker and `pvp_legal` case from `ARENA-Q-RULES` (teleport in, spawn one opponent at a time that fights under PvP rules (not a hired fighter and not a monster) and forfeits loot, XP and gold; full heal of health and mana after each win; countdown between matches; resets on loss, death rules, disconnect or leaving). The server computes everything; the client only sends the accept/claim actions. Saved state must be `#[serde(default)]`. The run must not touch the live PvP matches in the Arena and must work with several players in separate runs at once.
- [x] `ARENA-Q-DATA` — **Done 2026-10-21; merged and released.** One quest per level 20, 30, ..., 90 in `world/map.txt` through a generator script (seeded, byte-stable), each with `level`, `rewardXp`, gold and a `rewardChoices` blue weapon per class (via `scripts/quest_gear_rewards.py` or the weapon ladder; the weapon must exist at that level in `world/items.txt`, regenerate with `gear_levels.py`, never hand-edit). Quest ids globally unique, accept string within 32 characters. Hand-written Swedish for the quest text, giver lines, countdown and result messages in the `sv-*.txt` files; run `build_swedish.py`.
- [x] `ARENA-Q-CLIENT` — **Done 2026-10-21; merged and released.** Quest journal entry and progress ("Fight 2 of 5"), the opponent's name and class shown, a countdown banner between matches, a result message after each win or loss, claim button in step with the NPC (journal and gossip claim buttons must stay in step; a full bag pays nothing and keeps the quest ready). Add any new window or HUD piece to `WINDOWS`/`ITEMS`/`EXTRA` in `settings.js`.
- [x] `ARENA-Q-CHECK` — **Done 2026-10-21; merged and released.** 6 Rust tests, the Chromium check `tests/gauntlet-ui.cjs` (13 checks), the MCP scenario `gauntlet` (levels 20 and 99, 24 checks; `scripts/testing/gauntlet-scenario.cjs`, plus a `pvp` action in the test driver) and the measured win rates in `docs/arena-quests.md`. Original task: Focused checks of the new thing only: a Rust test for the run, an MCP scenario for the whole quest at levels 20 and 99, and one Chromium check of the journal, countdown and claim. Balance check with the duel harness: report win rates per class and level in `docs/arena-quests.md`; do not re-tune PvP constants.

- [x] `AMBIENT-CHAT-MORE` — **Done 2026-10-22:** `world/ambient-chat.txt` (field 134, town 88, 204 new lines plus the 18 old ones; `LineBag` shuffled bag per adventurer and place), named timing constants at the top of `ambient.rs` (first line 3-5 min, then 6-10 min, one line per zone per 60 s). **Owner, 2026-10-22: the lines are NOT translated** (no Swedish entries, no client change). Tests: `chat_lines_are_short_unique_and_plentiful`, `a_line_bag_never_repeats_until_the_list_is_used_up`, `adventurers_speak_rarely_and_a_zone_hears_one_at_a_time` (mutation-checked). Today there are only 18 lines (`FIELD_CHAT` 10, `TOWN_CHAT` 8) and each adventurer speaks every 120-240 s (`next_chat`, first line after 45-120 s) whenever a real player is within 18 tiles, so the same few lines repeat constantly. **Sentences:** about 200 new ones in total, written by hand (no machine text), split by place (field and town at least; consider dungeon-door, hub, night and weather lines if they are cheap, since the zone and time of day are known), short, in the voice of adventurers, never naming a real zone, quest or boss that may not exist where they say it, no instructions that sound like game rules, nothing that a real player could mistake for a staff announcement. Move the lists out of the source into a data file (for example `world/ambient-chat.txt`, JSON array per place, embedded like the other world tables) so adding lines is not a Rust change. Pick a line the adventurer has not said lately (a short per-adventurer memory, or a shuffled bag), so one person never repeats while the rest of the list is unused. **Frequency:** lower it clearly (owner: "decrease the frequency of talking"); **decided by the owner (2026-10-21): 6-10 minutes between lines per adventurer instead of 2-4, first line after 3-5 minutes, and a zone-wide cap of one ambient line per 60 s so three adventurers never talk at once**, keep them silent in combat as today, and make the interval constants named values at the top of the file. **Swedish:** every new line needs its hand-written Swedish in the `sv-*.txt` files (chat from the server reaches the client as plain text and `Locale.translate` matches it exactly); run `python3 scripts/build_swedish.py`, and the 200 lines must pass `npm run test:swedish`. **Check (new thing only):** a Rust test that the data file parses, every line is non-empty, unique and short (say at most 90 characters), every list has a floor of lines, and that the pick never repeats until the list is used up; a Rust test of the new interval (a seeded world advances time and counts the lines said by three adventurers); no whole-suite run. Wiki page `10_projects:valhallasc_ambient_chat_<date>`.

A hidden GM (`is_hidden`) cannot be inspected; mercenaries and escorts answer as adventurers do (same fields, marked "Hired fighter"); an adventurer's gear is whatever it really wears, never invented for the window. **Window:** `#inspect-panel` as a new modal above z-index 49 (add it to `WINDOWS` in `settings.js` so it scales and resizes), the character drawn with the real `MageSprite` layers for that look and gear (as the character window does), the equipment slots as icons with hover/focus tooltips matching the inventory's, the stats beside them, live refresh while it stays open (re-request every few seconds or on an equip event, and say "No longer nearby" when the target left), Escape closes, and it works at 390x844. ### Bind on pickup (owner's request, 2026-10-21)

- [x] `TRADE-BOUND-GEAR` — **Done 2026-10-22 (committed; the client files are live, the server rule needs the next release binary).** Class pieces are refused in `validate_offer` (offer, confirm and settle); a Need/Greed/Pass prize carries a 7,200 s wall-clock window for the other voters (`GearInstance.trade_until`/`trade_with`), one hand-off; the picker greys bound pieces, tooltips say "Bound on pickup", the roll end names the window; hand-written Swedish. Checks: `cargo test trade` and `rolls` (mutation-checked), `tests/trade-bound-ui.cjs` (9 checks, `MUTATE=1` fails), clippy, fmt, Swedish gates. Wiki `10_projects:valhallasc_bind_on_pickup_20261022`. Original task: Class gear pieces are bind on pickup: owner, "you can't trade them to someone else, only sell". **Today:** `validate_offer` in `server/src/world/trade.rs` already refuses starter gear, game-master gear, mount licences (`i.kind == "mount_licence"`) and quest items (`is_quest_item`), each with its own message; nothing stops a class piece. **Rule:** an item that is gear (`is_gear`) and has a class (`Item.class` is `Some`, 2,280 catalog entries) cannot be offered in a trade, whatever its rarity, level or source (drop, roll, quest reward, vendor, gauntlet weapon, orange). Selling to a merchant stays as it is, and so do equipping, refining and merging. **Existing gear:** every copy already in anyone's bags or bank is bound at once, with no migration (owner, 2026-10-21), so the base rule needs no flag: it reads the catalog item. **One exception, decided by the owner (2026-10-21): a rolled piece has a two-hour trade window.** A class piece that a Need/Greed/Pass roll awarded (`finish_roll` in `world/rolls.rs`; also a boss piece rolled the same way) can be traded, within two hours of being awarded, to a party member who was a voter in that roll (so a mistaken Need can be passed on, nobody else can receive it); after two hours, or once it is traded once, it is bound like any other. (Confirmed by the owner, 2026-10-21: real time, a logout does not pause it, and one hand-off only.) That needs per-copy state, because the base rule is on the item type: add to the gear-instance ledger (`Character.gear_instances`; a plain catalog piece with no record gets one when the roll awards it, as `materialize_gear_instances` already does for old copies) a `trade_until` game time and a `trade_with` list of character ids, both `#[serde(default)]` (saves are one-way migrations), expiry judged against the server's clock (a logout does not stop it; use wall-clock seconds saved with the character, not `World.time`, which resets on a restart), cleared when the piece is traded, sold, merged or refined. Pieces that arrive any other way (a solo drop, a quest reward, the gauntlet, a vendor, a ground pile picked up) get no window. The trade window and the tooltip show the time left ("Tradeable for 1 h 42 min with the party of the roll") and the roll window's end message says which pieces have it. **Class-neutral gear** (class `None`: the one-time quest gear, `orange` class-neutral families, set pieces without a class) **stays tradeable (owner, 2026-10-21)**; orange class pieces from the raid are sell-only like every class piece (owner, 2026-10-21). Need/Greed/Pass among party members is unchanged apart from the window above; do not touch the other loot rules, the bank (a character's own storage) or mercenaries (they forfeit loot as today). **Server:** one new refusal in `validate_offer`, shared with the existing "cannot be traded" family, naming the item ("Class gear is bound to its owner and can only be sold."); keep the check inside the function that both sides' offers pass through, so a modified client cannot offer a piece (the server already re-validates at settle: add the same check in `settle_trade`'s validation path if it re-reads items separately). **Client:** the trade window's item picker (`client/trade.js`) shows a bound piece greyed out with the reason as its tooltip instead of letting the click through to a server error; item tooltips (`client/inventory.js`, the bank, the roll window) get a line "Bound on pickup" / its hand-written Swedish for class gear only; add the Swedish for the new refusal and tooltip to the `sv-*.txt` files and run `python3 scripts/build_swedish.py`. **Check (new thing only):** a Rust test that a trade offering a class piece (no window) of each of the five classes and of every rarity present in the catalog is refused (and a class-neutral piece, a material and a potion are still accepted), that the other side cannot add it either, that selling one to a merchant still works, that a rolled piece can be traded to a voter of its roll inside two hours and to nobody else, and not after two hours (advance the saved clock) or a second time, that the window survives a save and reload, and that a trade with a bound piece offered by a hand-built message is refused at settle; mutation-check by removing the new condition. One Chromium check that the picker greys a class piece with its tooltip and still offers a neutral item. Docs: a line in the trade section of the wiki page and `AGENTS.md` ("class gear is untradeable, sell only"). Wiki page `10_projects:valhallasc_bind_on_pickup_<date>`.

### Raid key: ten Firelord crosses (owner's request, 2026-10-24)

Owner's brief, verbatim: "the raid is gonna have a quest to be able to enter. in 10 different zones. make a human sized cross. like a grave. that when you have the quest it summons a firelord or something and you have to get 10 pieces of a key. and the firelords drop a piece each. greenmeadow should definitely have a cross. grill me". **Decided in a grill round (2026-10-24):**
- **Door and key:** the Chained Forge's outer door gets `needs_quest: raid_key` (`Portal.needs_quest`, checked per hero in `use_portal`, as the Foundry's `null_keys` does). Completing the quest is a **permanent unlock** for that hero; the ten pieces are quest items (`material`, `sell: 0` = `items::is_quest_item`: unsellable, untradeable, kept after the quest). Follow `world/hour_shards.rs`. Hired mercenaries follow their hirer and need nothing.
- **Quest giver:** a new NPC at the Emberwake arrival camp (zone 45), offered from **level 99** (`min_level` 99). The quest wants ten pieces, one per cross zone. Hand-written Swedish for every text. Name the quest and the pieces in the first task; skip any name that already exists in `items.txt`/`map.txt`.
- **Ten crosses, one per zone, outdoor zones in level order (owner's choice):** Greenmeadow (required), Emberfall Crags, Rimeveil, Gloamfen, Wyrdwood, Bifrost Reach, Ran's Deep, Astralhollow, Prismwaste, Obsidian Orrery. **Rán's Deep stays (owner, second grill round 2026-10-24)**: put its cross on a seabed ledge. The zones are low level and the Firelords are level 99, so for a level-99 hero this is a tour of old zones.
- **The cross:** a human-sized grave cross, scenery with an interaction (the Key map action for "interact"; no letter in text, `Keys.is`). Inert (no prompt) for heroes without the quest and for heroes who already hold that zone's piece. Place each cross in a quiet spot of its zone, away from camps, hubs and portal arrivals (no enemy within 8 units, outside every gate disc). Each cross gets art that matches its zone's tone; the Greenmeadow cross must be easy to find.
- **The Firelord:** summoned when a hero with the quest and no piece for that zone interacts. **One Firelord kind, no unique mechanics** (a plain strong elite; name and tint may follow the zone, no new sprite set per zone), **level 99, tuned so a strong lone player can win** (a lone hero in purple gear beats it; a party does it quickly), full-heal on evade, soft enrage like other elites (add it to `world/enrage.txt` and extend the `matches!(kind, ...)` full-heal match in `world.rs`). It is a **shared world boss**: everyone nearby sees it and can fight it. Pick the ten names and the level-99 stat row in `world/elite-power.txt`; **do not run balance sweeps** (owner measures; see the Molten raid note).
- **Piece drop:** every hero **with the quest, who still lacks that zone's piece and who fought within `SHARE_RANGE` (60)** gets their own private piece when it dies (private drop, so nobody can steal it; no roll). Heroes who already hold the piece can help but get nothing. The cross is usable again at once by anyone who still needs the piece, so there is no global cooldown. At most one Firelord per cross at a time. **Despawn (owner, second round): it chases until killed** (`Slime.hunt`, no leash); it goes away only when it dies or no hero is left in the zone, and then the cross re-arms. Implementation guard, not asked: it must never enter a safe hub or sanctuary (stop at city bounds / `pvp_legal` sanctuary rules) and must not follow through portals; report to the owner if that conflicts.
- **Look (owner, second round): the raid's Eldmarr art, scaled down**, as the Eldmarr's herald (the owner accepts that it shows Eldmarr's look early); per-zone tint and a smaller size, no new PixelFlow set. **Third grill round (2026-10-24):** (1) **Finding crosses: the journal lists only the ten zone names**, no world-map or mini-map markers, no tracker arrow (a treasure hunt; the Greenmeadow cross must still be easy to find, and no cross may sit behind fog-of-war-only access). (2) **Summon: a 3 s channel at the cross, no cost**; damage or moving interrupts it, the cross glows during the channel, then the Firelord rises; an interrupted channel starts nothing and the cross stays armed. (3) **Loot: the private key piece only**, no gold, XP, gear or elite table (set the Firelord's `gold`/XP to zero or route it through the quest-item path; `items::is_elite` applies, so check it does not roll the elite drop table). (4) **Names: Ember Shards and the Ember Key**: ten shards (one per zone, named after the zone, for example `Greenmeadow Ember Shard`) join into the Ember Key; the quest is "The Ember Key". Swedish by hand (glossary in `docs/swedish-glossary.md`). **Not decided (decide in the first task, do not ask again unless it is new):** the interaction prompt wording; the quest tracker line "Pieces: n/10" is expected; whether ambient adventurers or mercenaries may help (default: they may, but never count for credit); the Swedish; the quest and piece names; a short fire-themed boss sting or reuse of an existing boss music cue (a new song only if the owner asks).
- **Tasks (chain: rules, then data and server, then client, then check, then release):**
  - [x] `RAID-KEY-RULES` — **Done 2026-10-24: `docs/raid-key.md` records all decisions, names, Swedish and checked coordinates.** Write `docs/raid-key.md` from this entry and settle the "Not decided" list. Include the zone/cross coordinates for the ten zones (read `world/map.txt`, never guess) and the quest and item ids. Wiki `10_projects:valhallasc_raid_key_<date>`.
  - [x] `RAID-KEY-SERVER` — **Built 2026-10-24; eight focused Rust tests and the nine-piece gate mutation pass.** Quest, ten piece items (`material`, `sell: 0`), the Firelord kind and its `elite-power.txt`/`enrage.txt` rows, cross objects in `map.txt` (zone anatomy: add a `crosses` list, generator-owned so they survive reruns of every zone generator in the chain), the summon op, shared-boss credit and per-hero piece drop (`world/raid_key.rs`, modelled on `hour_shards.rs`), and `needs_quest: raid_key` on the Chained Forge door. New file zone: none. Run the generators, `node scripts/sync-world.cjs`, rebuild. Rust tests for the new thing only: the drop reaches every eligible hero and nobody else, an inert cross refuses, the door refuses without the quest, the summon is one per cross, the despawn re-arms. Mutation-check the door gate.
  - [x] `RAID-KEY-CLIENT` — **Built 2026-10-24: original PixelFlow cross, smaller tinted Eldmarr herald, remappable Interact (G), tracker, Swedish and login release notice. Seventeen real-sprite browser checks pass; `MUTATE=1` fails.** Cross art (one human-sized sprite, per-zone tint is enough), the interaction prompt, the Firelord's look (Eldmarr's art scaled down and tinted per zone; check `client/` for how the boss sprite is loaded and size the health bar `top` to the smaller figure), quest tracker and journal, Swedish by hand, `Keys` action for the interaction. Check with one real-sprite browser test (`tests/raid-key-ui.cjs`, `MUTATE=1` must fail): the Greenmeadow cross appears, is inert without the quest, summons with it, the piece shows in the bags and the tracker counts it.
  - [x] `RAID-KEY-CHECK` — **Done 2026-10-24: `raid_key` MCP scenario passes 11 checks; Clippy/fmt, byte stability, JS/Python syntax and the full Swedish gate pass. No grinding or balance sweep.** One MCP scenario `raid_key`: a level-99 hero takes the quest at the Emberwake camp, summons one Firelord, kills it with `kill_enemy`, receives the piece, then the door refuses at nine pieces and admits at ten. No grinding, no timing sweeps.
  - [x] `RAID-KEY-OPEN` — **Released 2026-10-24 06:10 UTC, source `bd16999`, with nobody online. Backup `deploy/backups/raid-key-20261024T055838Z`; public/bridge health, all 13 public asset hashes, running binary, SQLite quick_check, unchanged character/guest counts and no test flags/journal warnings verified. The release also includes the autoattack fixes. Nobody grandfathered; no backfill or zone-ID shift. Wiki `10_projects:valhallasc_raid_key_20261024`.** Release: nobody online, backup, binary and client together (the server embeds `map.txt`/`items.txt`), verify health, hashes and that a character without the key is refused at the door. **Nobody is grandfathered (owner, second round)**: every hero, including past raid clearers, does the quest; no backfill. Warn in the release notice, and make sure a hero already inside a raid copy when the server restarts is not stranded (saved-in-copy characters wake at the door).

### Molten raid: 10-player dungeon (owner's request, 2026-10-21)

**Owner testing decision (2026-10-23):** the owner will test encounter times and win rates personally. Do not run repeated timing, win-rate or multi-seed balance sweeps unless explicitly requested. Keep agent validation focused on mechanics and integration. This supersedes the older 16-seed raid timing instructions. Existing measurements are historical evidence, not a requirement for further runs.

Owner's brief, verbatim: "Create a 10 man dungeon that is 4 times the size of a regular dungeon. packs take 4 times as long to kill and bosses take 4 times as long to kill. 10 bosses. all of them with unique mechanics. and a final boss that is the main antagonist in the Valhalla universe. he should take at least 15 minutes to kill and drop all orange gear. the other bosses drops orange gear also. so all the classes can get full orange gear. the final boss drops the weapons which have extra cool features. the music should be techno the estetica of the dungeon should be underground molten core ish". Working name **the Molten Core** (ids `RAID-*`, zone name to be settled in `RAID-RULES`). Use the `createmap` skill for the zone; the chain is `RAID-RULES` first, then `RAID-PARTY`, `RAID-APPROACH`, `RAID-MAP`, `RAID-PACKS`, the boss tasks, `RAID-ORANGE`, `RAID-WEAPONS`, `RAID-MUSIC`, then `RAID-CHECK` and `RAID-OPEN`. The entrance stays sealed until `RAID-OPEN` is done. Owner preferences that apply: test only the new thing (one boss at a time, one scratch test binary, no wide suites), never `pkill -f` a pattern in your own command line, hand-written Swedish, new outdoor zones get three elites (two with quests, one without); this dungeon has elites in every pack and no special elite quests (owner, 2026-10-22), a new music style, no public restart or database migration without the owner's go-ahead.

- [x] `RAID-RULES` — **Done on `feat/raid-approach` (design commit `c7c769a`; current source `7fa349b`, not merged/deployed).** Approved design and sixth-round answers are in that branch's `docs/molten-raid.md`. **Original brief:** Write `docs/molten-raid.md` from the owner's brief and settle what is open; ask the owner the questions below in one round and record the answers in the doc (do not guess). **Read first:** `docs/blackhour.md`, `docs/boss-mechanics.md`, `docs/orange.md`, `docs/ttk-plan.md`, `docs/nornroot.md`. **Interpretations to confirm:** (a) "10 bosses" = ten in total, nine ordinary bosses plus the final one (the main antagonist), not ten plus a final; (b) level 99, because only that gives a hard target for full orange gear (the `Worldheart` family is level 99); (c) "4 times the size" = four times the walkable floor area **and** four times the number of packs and rooms of the biggest existing dungeon (measure Black Hour Foundry, Cathedral wing and Longship from `world/map.txt`: floor cells, packs, bosses; write the baseline numbers in the doc); (d) "4 times as long to kill" means 4x the measured kill time of the same kind of fight in a normal dungeon, **judged with ten players**, so health must scale by about 8x (ten players deal twice the damage), not 4x; the doc states the baseline times (non-final bosses are tuned to ~70 s for the hired five; Black Hour packs 125-156 s with blue gear, side bosses 204-231 s, final 386-571 s) and the target for each pack kind and boss; the final boss takes at least 900 s (15 min) with ten players in full orange gear, so measure with orange, not blue. **Open questions for the owner:** who is the main antagonist (no lore page names one: the docs, wiki and `client/` have no "main villain", so the owner names him, with a sentence of backstory and where he is first seen/mentioned, so a sign or NPC in the hubs can foreshadow him); where the entrance is (which hub or zone, and the entry level: 99 only, or from level 90 like a prestige tier); who may enter (a 10-player group only, or also 6-9 with the same boss health, or a scaled raid); whether it resets on a timer or on wipe, and whether there is a weekly lockout on the orange drops; what "extra cool features" on the weapons means (see `RAID-WEAPONS`); whether orange drops are guaranteed per boss or rolled from a pool, and whether the loot is personal (each hero gets their own) or rolled Need/Greed/Pass among ten (the brief's "so all the classes can get full orange gear" implies a guaranteed route to every slot of every class: decide the number of kills it should take). **Also settle:** mercenaries (ten-player fights cannot be tested with a hired five: decide how many hired fighters are allowed per hirer and how the tests stand in for ten; AGENTS.md says party tests use the hired fighters only, never the retired bot party), the ten-player XP and gold split, the death rule (final bosses keep the fallen down, `BossMech::fight_in`; keep it for a raid), enrage per boss, the zone id (next free file zone; adding a file zone shifts every private-copy id, so record the shift in the doc and recheck saved characters before any release; see the Black Hour and Longship notes in AGENTS.md), and the copy count (a raid is long, so two or three copies, not four).
  **Decided by the owner (2026-10-21, first round):** (1) the main antagonist is a **Surtr-style fire giant** (the owner still gives his name and backstory; the doc proposes two or three names and a sentence each from the Norse fire-giant myth, kept original, and lists where he is foreshadowed in the hubs); (2) entry is **level 99 only, exactly ten players** (no smaller groups, no scaled health; the entrance place is still open, propose Yearwheel 28 or a hub); (3) loot is **Need/Greed/Pass among the ten** (the existing 30 s roll, `rolls.rs`, extended to ten rollers). Because a roll alone cannot promise every class a full set, `RAID-ORANGE` must still guarantee enough drops per class slot across the ten bosses and the doc states the expected kills for a full set per class; (4) the final boss weapons get all three feature kinds: a proc effect, a new or changed skill while equipped, and a visual and sound identity (see `RAID-WEAPONS`). **Decided (second round, same day):** (5) the entrance is a **door in a new volcanic zone** (see `RAID-APPROACH`; which hub it hangs off is still open), not an existing zone; (6) **no lockout**: the raid can be rerun at will, every kill drops, the instance resets on a wipe or after a clear; (7) **a hirer can hire nine mercenaries** for the raid ("for this you should be able to hire 9"), so a ten-player fight is the hirer plus nine mercenaries driven by the real merc brain (`world/mercs.rs`, `boss_play.rs`) and the tests need no stand-in; the doc must say whether the nine are allowed only inside the raid (assumed, with the 250-gold-to-`merc_price` hire at the Meeting Stone at the raid door) or everywhere, and how the hire price, the XP and gold split (a mercenary's share is destroyed) and the loot rule (mercenaries forfeit loot; they roll Need only for class upgrades today) work with nine; the mercenary cap constant moves from four to nine for the raid zone only unless the owner says otherwise; (8) **three private copies**. **Decided (third round, same day):** (9) the ten are **any mix of humans and hired mercenaries, ten in total** (1 human + 9 mercenaries, 4 + 6, or 10 + 0); every human may hire into the free slots, and the group never exceeds ten (the group frame, the portal's `players: 10` gate and the hire check all count mercenaries); (10) **mercenaries never roll**: Need/Greed/Pass is among the humans only (a roll with one human is awarded to that human at once, no 30 s wait), a mercenary's share of a drop goes into the humans' rolls, matching today's "mercenaries forfeit loot" rule; this means a solo hirer gets every drop, so `RAID-ORANGE` computes kills-to-full-set for the solo case and for ten humans; (11) the nine-mercenary cap is **raid only** (the volcanic zone and every other dungeon stay at the current cap); (12) the volcanic zone's door hangs off **Asterion** (zone 17, the safe hub with services and Spark Travel), and the antagonist is called **Eldmarr** (owner's pick of the proposed names; the doc gives a one-sentence backstory for the owner to approve, and a foreshadowing line in Asterion). **Decided (fourth round, same day):** (13) health is **fixed for ten fighters**: mercenaries count as fighters, so one human with nine mercenaries and ten humans face the same fight, no scaling by group makeup; (14) **all kill-time targets are measured with the hirer plus nine mercenaries** (the real `auto_play` brain, full orange gear); skilled humans may be faster and that is accepted, so the 15 minute final boss floor and the 4x pack and boss times are mercenary-group times, and the doc records the median and fastest of at least 16 seeds; the lone-player and short-group controls (ten minus two must lose or take clearly longer) use the same fixture; (15) **a wipe resets only the current boss to full health, killed bosses stay dead**; the fallen keep today's final-boss rule while the fight runs (they stay down, `BossMech::fight_in`), and when the whole group has fallen everyone wakes at the door of that boss's hall and the boss and its helpers reset (the doc states the 60 s grace for returning and whether the sleeping helpers of `place_boss_adds.py` are re-placed); (16) ordinary packs are **hard but skippable in part**: the path to each boss is mandatory, but some packs can be pulled one at a time or sneaked past and none is behind a lock that needs the whole pack dead; the doc marks which packs are mandatory (the 4x kill time applies to every pack kind either way). **Decided (fifth round, same day):** (17) **Eldmarr's backstory (owner: "you come up with it"; written by the agent, original, goes into `docs/molten-raid.md`, the sign texts and the final boss's lines in hand-written Swedish):** before Valhalla had a roof, Eldmarr, the Ember-Lord of the fire giants, kept the first forge beneath the world; its heat kept the roots of the world tree alive through the long winter, and the gods raised their hall on his fire. They promised him a seat at the table and never gave it. When he came up to claim it they bound him in the stone under Asterion's mountain with nine chains, each held by a Warden (the nine ordinary bosses, once his own smiths and captains, set to guard him in return for a place in the hall), and they told the world the forge had gone out. It had not: the chains hold the fire in, and the fire has been rising for a hundred years. The Wardens have gone slag-mad from the heat, or have started to feed him, one by one the chains are failing, and the ground around Asterion is warm in the evenings. Eldmarr does not want to burn the world for its own sake; he wants the table he was promised, and he will take the hall by melting the roots that hold it. The heroes descend to break the Wardens before the last chain gives and then end him (the final boss). Foreshadowing (round 5, in Asterion, hand-written Swedish): warm flagstones and a cracked well in the square, a smith NPC who says the old forge is "only sleeping", a sign at the volcanic zone's portal naming the nine chains, one line from Eldmarr in the last quest of the approach zone. The Wardens' names and one-line mechanics-themed identities (chain, ember, bellows, anvil, slag and so on) are proposed in this doc, one per boss task. (18) **Packs drop purple at 3% per kill** (owner, 2026-10-21): every ordinary raid enemy has a 3% chance to drop a purple piece of level 99 (today an ordinary enemy's `epic` base is 0.01%, `base_drop_chance` in `items.rs`, so this is 300 times higher; the outdoor-elite rule is 5%), as an independent roll in addition to the normal table, class-appropriate pool at level 99 from the existing purple ladder (not the new orange family), and through `copies()`/`loot_stack`/`pile_for` so the rebirth multiplier still applies; the doc states the expected purple per full clear for the ten-fighter group and whether it counts per enemy or per pack (assumed: per enemy). (19) the volcanic zone's music is **dub-techno / half-time**, about 110 BPM (see `RAID-APPROACH`). **Nothing else is open in the owner's brief**; the Wardens' names, the raid's level layout and the per-class weapon procs are proposed by the agent in `docs/molten-raid.md` for approval.

### Death: durability and repair instead of XP loss (owner's request, 2026-10-22)

Owner's words, verbatim: "remove exp penalty when dying. introduce gear durability and repair instead". **Today:** `Player::apply_death_penalty` (`server/src/world.rs`) calls `Character::lose_death_xp` (`server/src/model.rs`): a death takes `xp_need/3` and can drop the level; the notice is "You lost {lost} XP." and `docs`/AGENTS.md describe it under "Death". Tasks chain `*-RULES` first, then SERVER, CLIENT, CHECK. Not started, nothing decided beyond the two sentences above.

- [x] `DEATH-RULES` — **Done 2026-10-23: `docs/durability.md` written from the owner's two rounds.** **Original task:** Write `docs/durability.md` from the owner's brief and ask the owner the open questions in one round (`AskUserQuestion`, 2-4 decisions at a time) before any code; record the answers in this entry the same turn. **To settle:** (a) what wears gear down: dying only, or also hits taken and blows dealt (and PvP: duels and the Arena lose no durability, as they lose no XP today); (b) the unit: a per-copy `durability`/`max_durability` on the gear-instance ledger (`GearInstance`, `#[serde(default)]`, an old copy starts full, like `trade_until`), how much one death costs (a percentage of max, per piece or only the worn ones), and whether the amount grows with level or rarity; (c) a broken piece: stat penalty scaled by condition, or zero stats at 0 and still worn, never destroyed or unequippable (the owner has not asked for loss of gear); (d) repair: which NPC and where (every safe hub's smith? a repair button in the bag window next to a vendor?), the price formula (by level and rarity, with a "repair all" button), whether materials or crystals are needed, and whether a repair is allowed in combat or mounted; (e) which slots have durability (all nine, the cloak when it lands, class-neutral and orange pieces, GM gear never, starter gear never) and whether weapons wear differently from armour; (f) mercenaries (they wear gear: no durability, or repaired free), the Arena gauntlet fighters and raid fixtures (no durability in tests unless asked); (g) the existing `lose_death_xp` test and level-drop text go away, and what the death notice says instead; (h) saved characters: no migration, copies without the fields count as full; (i) interaction with `TRADE-BOUND-GEAR`, gear merge/refine (does a refined or merged copy keep its condition?), the bank and the Swedish glossary words (`Durability` / `Hållbarhet`, `Repair` / `Reparera`).
  **Decided by the owner (2026-10-22, round 1):** (a) **only dying wears gear**: every death damages the worn pieces, combat and hits taken wear nothing, and duels and the Arena cost nothing (as they cost no XP today). (b) A broken piece: **full stats at any durability above 0, zero stats at 0** (a cliff, not a scale); it stays worn and is never lost or destroyed. (c) One death takes **10% of the maximum from every worn piece** (ten deaths break a full set), the same at every level and rarity; the price of repair scales with level and rarity instead. (d) Repair is at **a smith in every safe hub** (Alderhaven, Skaldholm, Nacrehold, Asterion), a Repair button on each piece plus **Repair all** with the total price, gold only, not in combat or mounted.
  **Decided by the owner (2026-10-22, round 2):** (e) **Price**: repairing from 0 to full costs about **25% of the piece's sale value** (`saleValue`), scaled linearly by the missing durability, so level and rarity are followed with no new table; Repair all sums the pieces. (f) **Slots**: every worn gear slot (weapon, armour, necklace, accessories, the cloak when it lands), every rarity including orange and class-neutral; **starter and game master gear never wear**. (g) **Mercenaries and ambient adventurers have no durability**, the kill-time and boss harnesses keep full condition; only the hirer's own gear wears. (h) **Condition travels with the copy** (the bank and a trade keep it); **merge and refine restore the result to full**. Left to the build (no owner round needed unless a snag appears): the low-durability HUD hint and the exact death-notice wording (`DEATH-CLIENT`), the exact "sale value" rounding (whole gold, at least 1 for a damaged piece).
